My current understanding:

- MFP eliminates neural-network derivative contractions layer by layer using exact conditional Wick–Stein identities, while preserving Onsager responses, equality partitions, and global width counting. The intended output is an explicit Gaussian-normal-form DAG. See [CURRENT_RESEARCH_STATE.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/CURRENT_RESEARCH_STATE.md:16).
- The local Gaussian calculus and several specializations are exact, but the grammar-wide compiler theorem, general concentration, depth-independent finite-state closure, and shared \(O(L)\) DAG remain open.

- The positive-time picture is nuanced: linear identity networks are proved through exactly three hidden layers; the nonlinear two-hidden-layer arctangent IDE is proved; the canonical raw-square continuous-readout conjecture is disproved; and the nonzero-label depth-three tanh theorem remains open.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 535 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.646Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes—the one-step, finite-learning-rate calculation closes exactly.

For
\[
u_i=\frac{w_i^\top x}{\sqrt{d_0}},\qquad
q_0=\frac{\|x\|^2}{d_0},\qquad
f_n=\frac1n\sum_i a_i\phi(u_i),
\]
take the simultaneous μP feature-ascent step
\[
\theta^+=\theta+\eta n\nabla_\theta f_n.
\]
Then
\[
a_i^+=a_i+\eta\phi(u_i),\qquad
u_i^+=u_i+\eta q_0a_i\phi'(u_i).
\]

Hence, with independent \(A\sim N(0,1)\) and \(U\sim N(0,q_0)\),
\[
\boxed{
\mathbb E f_n^+
=
\mathbb E\!\left[
\bigl(A+\eta\phi(U)\bigr)
\phi\!\left(U+\eta q_0A\phi'(U)\right)
\right].
}
\]

This is exact for every \(n\), not merely asymptotic. Under integrability, \(f_n^+\) also converges almost surely to this expectation.

Gaussian integration by parts gives the useful cross-kernel form
\[
\boxed{
\mathbb E f_n^+
=
\eta\,\mathbb E\!\left[
\phi(U)\phi(U^+)
+q_0\phi'(U)\phi'(U^+)
\right],
\qquad
U^+=U+\eta q_0A\phi'(U).
}
\]

Thus the finite-\(\eta\) DAG is simply
\[
(A,U)\rightarrow(\phi(U),\phi'(U))
\rightarrow(A^+,U^+)
\rightarrow A^+\phi(U^+)
\rightarrow\mathbb E.
\]
It uses two Gaussian sources and remains finite-dimensional; unlike ordinary MFP, it retains the shifted nonlinear evaluation \(\phi(U^+)\).

Checks:

\[
\phi(u)=u:\qquad \mathbb E f_n^+=2\eta q_0,
\]

\[
\phi(u)=u^2:\qquad
\mathbb E f_n^+=7\eta q_0^2+12\eta^3q_0^4.
\]

The quadratic linear term matches MFP, but at \(q_0=1\) the continuous-flow expansion has cubic coefficient \(160\), whereas one Euler step has coefficient \(12\). Finite Euler motion therefore cannot be reconstructed by simply evaluating the initialization-flow jet at \(t=\eta\).

The complete derivation is in [README.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/README.md:46), with an evaluator in [finite_step_dag.py](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/finite_step_dag.py:1) and five passing checks in [test_finite_step_dag.py](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/test_finite_step_dag.py:1).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 579 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.648Z | assistant | final_answer ===
You’re right—the previous notation was unnecessarily elaborate. Here is the clean canonical version.

For one input $x\in\mathbb R^d$, define

$$
q=\frac{\|x\|^2}{d},
\qquad
z_i=\frac{w_i^\top x}{\sqrt d},
\qquad
f_n(x)=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n a_i\phi(z_i).
$$

Initialize $w_i\sim N(0,I_d)$ and $a_i\sim N(0,1)$ independently. Take one simultaneous μP feature-ascent step:

$$
(a,w)^+=(a,w)+\eta n\nabla_{a,w}f_n(x).
$$

The parameter updates are

$$
a_i^+=a_i+\eta\phi(z_i),
$$

$$
w_i^+=w_i+\eta a_i\phi'(z_i)\frac{x}{\sqrt d}.
$$

Therefore

$$
z_i^+
=\frac{(w_i^+)^\top x}{\sqrt d}
=z_i+\eta q\,a_i\phi'(z_i).
$$

Let $A\sim N(0,1)$ and $Z\sim N(0,q)$ be independent, and define

$$
Z_\eta=Z+\eta qA\phi'(Z).
$$

Then the expected post-step output is exactly

$$
F_\eta
:=\mathbb E[f_n^+(x)]
=
\mathbb E\left[
\left(A+\eta\phi(Z)\right)\phi(Z_\eta)
\right].
$$

This holds for every finite width $n$. Under integrability, $f_n^+(x)\to F_\eta$ almost surely as $n\to\infty$.

The entire computational DAG is

$$
(A,Z)
\longrightarrow
\left(A+\eta\phi(Z),\,Z_\eta\right)
\longrightarrow
\left(A+\eta\phi(Z)\right)\phi(Z_\eta)
\longrightarrow
\mathbb E.
$$

Gaussian integration by parts also gives

$$
F_\eta
=
\eta\,\mathbb E\left[
\phi(Z)\phi(Z_\eta)
+
q\phi'(Z)\phi'(Z_\eta)
\right].
$$

Thus the finite step is an old-state/new-state cross-kernel. For small $\eta$,

$$
F_\eta
=
\eta\,\mathbb E\left[\phi(Z)^2+q\phi'(Z)^2\right]
+O(\eta^3),
$$

recovering the initialization tangent kernel at first order.

Two exact checks are

$$
\phi(z)=z
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
F_\eta=2\eta q,
$$

and

$$
\phi(z)=z^2
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
F_\eta=7\eta q^2+12\eta^3q^4.
$$

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 724 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.653Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Assuming two simultaneous explicit-Euler ascent steps with the gradient recomputed after step one, define

$$
f_n(x)=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n a_i\phi(z_i),
\qquad
q=\frac{\lVert x\rVert^2}{d},
$$

with independent initialization

$$
a_0\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\qquad
z_0\sim\mathcal N(0,q).
$$

Each neuron follows

$$
a_{k+1}=a_k+\eta\phi(z_k),
\qquad
z_{k+1}=z_k+\eta q\,a_k\phi'(z_k),
\qquad k=0,1.
$$

Thus

$$
\begin{aligned}
a_1&=a_0+\eta\phi(z_0),\\
z_1&=z_0+\eta q\,a_0\phi'(z_0),\\
a_2&=a_1+\eta\phi(z_1),\\
z_2&=z_1+\eta q\,a_1\phi'(z_1).
\end{aligned}
$$

The important point is that $z_2$ uses $a_1$, not $a_2$.

Therefore the exact expected output after two steps is

$$
\boxed{
F_2=\mathbb E\!\left[a_2\phi(z_2)\right].
}
$$

Writing only in terms of the initialization, let

$$
u=z_0+\eta q\,a_0\phi'(z_0).
$$

Then

$$
\boxed{
F_2=
\mathbb E\!\left[
\left(a_0+\eta\phi(z_0)+\eta\phi(u)\right)
\phi\!\left(
u+\eta q\left(a_0+\eta\phi(z_0)\right)\phi'(u)
\right)
\right].
}
$$

This is exact for finite $\eta$; it is a two-dimensional Gaussian expectation, not an expansion around $\eta=0$.

The DAG is

$$
(a_0,z_0)
\longrightarrow
(a_1,z_1)
\longrightarrow
(a_2,z_2)
\longrightarrow
a_2\phi(z_2)
\longrightarrow
\mathbb E.
$$

No new Gaussian is introduced at step two. The pair $(a_1,z_1)$ is generally dependent and non-Gaussian.

As a check, for $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
\boxed{
F_2=4\eta q\left(1+\eta^2q\right).
}
$$

The $\eta^3$ term is a genuine finite-step effect. Consequently, two Euler steps are generally neither one frozen step of size $2\eta$ nor continuous feature flow evaluated at time $2\eta$.

I recorded the full result, including the optional Gaussian-integration-by-parts form, in [TWO_STEPS.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/TWO_STEPS.md). The evaluator and all eight tests pass in [finite_step_dag.py](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/finite_step_dag.py).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 907 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.659Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes. Let $\eta$ be the total step size and use

$$
h=\frac{\eta}{k}
$$

for each of the $k$ substeps. With $q=1$, initialize

$$
a_0,z_0\overset{\mathrm{ind}}{\sim}\mathcal N(0,1).
$$

The exact recursive DAG is

$$
\begin{aligned}
a_{s+1}&=a_s+h\phi(z_s),\\
z_{s+1}&=z_s+h\,a_s\phi'(z_s),
\end{aligned}
\qquad 0\le s<k.
$$

Every $(a_s,z_s)$ is a deterministic function of the same two Gaussian seeds $(a_0,z_0)$. Therefore,

$$
F_k(\eta)=\mathbb E\!\left[a_k\phi(z_k)\right].
$$

Equivalently, as an explicit Gaussian integral,

$$
F_k(\eta)
=
\frac{1}{2\pi}
\int_{\mathbb R^2}
a_k(a,z)\phi\!\left(z_k(a,z)\right)
e^{-(a^2+z^2)/2}\,da\,dz.
$$

The stochastic dimension remains two for every $k$.

For an elegant integration-by-parts recursion, define

$$
u_s=\frac{\partial a_s}{\partial a_0},
\qquad
v_s=\frac{\partial z_s}{\partial a_0},
\qquad
(u_0,v_0)=(1,0).
$$

Differentiating the state recursion gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
u_{s+1}
&=u_s+h\phi'(z_s)v_s,\\
v_{s+1}
&=v_s+h\left(
u_s\phi'(z_s)+a_s\phi''(z_s)v_s
\right).
\end{aligned}
$$

Also,

$$
a_k=a_0+h\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}\phi(z_s).
$$

Gaussian integration by parts in $a_0$ gives

$$
\mathbb E\!\left[a_0\phi(z_k)\right]
=
\mathbb E\!\left[\phi'(z_k)v_k\right].
$$

Hence the exact recursive formula is

$$
\boxed{
F_k(\eta)
=
h\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}
\mathbb E\!\left[\phi(z_s)\phi(z_k)\right]
+
\mathbb E\!\left[\phi'(z_k)v_k\right],
\qquad
h=\frac{\eta}{k}.
}
$$

All expectations are still taken only over the initial independent Gaussian pair $(a_0,z_0)$. The raw recursion requires $\phi\in C^1$ and integrability; the integration-by-parts form requires $\phi\in C^2$ with suitable integrability.

If $\phi(z)=c$ literally, then

$$
a_k=a_0+khc,
\qquad
z_k=z_0,
$$

and therefore

$$
\boxed{F_k(\eta)=khc^2=\eta c^2.}
$$

Thus every refinement gives exactly the same output for a constant activation.

As a nontrivial check, for $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
\boxed{
F_k(\eta)
=
\frac12
\left[
\left(1+\frac{\eta}{k}\right)^{2k}
-
\left(1-\frac{\eta}{k}\right)^{2k}
\right].
}
$$

If instead every step has learning rate $\eta$, simply replace $h=\eta/k$ by $h=\eta$ throughout.

The derivation is recorded in [K_STEPS.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/K_STEPS.md), with the recursive Gaussian evaluator in [finite_step_dag.py](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_single_hidden_mlp/finite_step_dag.py). All 10 tests pass.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 1309 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.670Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes. I interpret “RMS one” as initialization normalization,

$$
\mathbb E[\phi(G)^2]=1,
\qquad
G\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
$$

without renormalizing the activations after each update.

For two hidden layers,

$$
u_j=\frac{w_j^\top x}{\sqrt d},
\qquad
z_i=\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_jW_{ij}\phi(u_j),
\qquad
f_n=\frac1n\sum_i a_i\phi(z_i),
$$

and the $\mu$P output-ascent update is

$$
\theta^{s+1}=\theta^s+\eta n\nabla_\theta f_n^s.
$$

The exact finite-width parameter recursion is

$$
\begin{aligned}
a_i^{s+1}
&=a_i^s+\eta\phi(z_i^s),\\
W_{ij}^{s+1}
&=W_{ij}^s+\frac{\eta}{\sqrt n}
a_i^s\phi'(z_i^s)\phi(u_j^s),\\
u_j^{s+1}
&=u_j^s+\eta b_j^s\phi'(u_j^s),
\end{aligned}
$$

where

$$
b_j^s
=
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_i
W_{ij}^s a_i^s\phi'(z_i^s).
$$

### One step

Let

$$
d=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)^2].
$$

Take independent

$$
U\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\qquad
B\sim\mathcal N(0,d),
$$

and define

$$
U_1=U+\eta B\phi'(U).
$$

Set

$$
C=\mathbb E[\phi(U)\phi(U_1)],
\qquad
V=\mathbb E[\phi(U_1)^2],
\qquad
R=\mathbb E[\phi'(U)\phi'(U_1)].
$$

Let $(Z,Y)$ be centered Gaussian with

$$
\operatorname{Var}(Z)=1,
\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Y)=V,
\qquad
\operatorname{Cov}(Z,Y)=C,
$$

and let $A\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$ be independent. Then the mean-field one-step DAG is

$$
a_1=A+\eta\phi(Z),
$$

$$
z_1=Y+\eta(C+R)A\phi'(Z).
$$

Thus

$$
F_1=\mathbb E[a_1\phi(z_1)].
$$

Gaussian integration by parts in $A$ gives

$$
F_1
=
\eta\mathbb E\!\left[
\phi(Z)\phi(z_1)
+
(C+R)\phi'(Z)\phi'(z_1)
\right].
$$

The $R$ term is the response caused by using the same middle matrix forward and backward. Omitting it loses the entire first-layer feature contribution.

For small $\eta$,

$$
F_1
=
\eta(1+d+d^2)+O(\eta^3).
$$

### Two steps

The clean recursive representation uses

$$
h_s=\phi(u_s),
\qquad
c_s=a_s\phi'(z_s),
$$

and the two covariance matrices

$$
Q_{sr}=\mathbb E[h_sh_r],
\qquad
K_{sr}=\mathbb E[c_sc_r].
$$

Introduce independent centered Gaussian processes $(\xi_s)$ and $(\chi_s)$ with

$$
\mathbb E[\xi_s\xi_r]=Q_{sr},
\qquad
\mathbb E[\chi_s\chi_r]=K_{sr}.
$$

Define the response coefficients

$$
\alpha_{sr}
=
\mathbb E\!\left[
\frac{\partial h_s}{\partial\chi_r}
\right],
\qquad r<s,
$$

$$
\beta_{sr}
=
\mathbb E\!\left[
\frac{\partial c_s}{\partial\xi_r}
\right],
\qquad r\le s.
$$

Then the two-sided Gaussian recursion is

$$
z_s
=
\xi_s+
\sum_{r<s}
\left(\alpha_{sr}+\eta Q_{sr}\right)c_r,
$$

$$
b_s
=
\chi_s+\beta_{ss}h_s
+
\sum_{r<s}
\left(\beta_{sr}+\eta K_{sr}\right)h_r,
$$

followed by

$$
a_{s+1}=a_s+\eta\phi(z_s),
\qquad
u_{s+1}=u_s+\eta b_s\phi'(u_s).
$$

Starting from

$$
u_0=U,
\qquad
a_0=A,
\qquad
z_0=\xi_0,
$$

this recursion constructs $(a_1,z_1)$ and then $(a_2,z_2)$. The second expected output is

$$
F_2=\mathbb E[a_2\phi(z_2)].
$$

It also has a compact Gaussian-integration-by-parts form. Put

$$
L_{sr}=\alpha_{sr}+\eta Q_{sr},
$$

and define

$$
p_0=\phi'(z_0),
\qquad
j_1=L_{10}p_0,
$$

$$
p_1=\phi'(z_1)+a_1\phi''(z_1)j_1,
$$

$$
j_2=L_{20}p_0+L_{21}p_1.
$$

Then

$$
F_2
=
\eta\mathbb E\!\left[
\bigl(\phi(z_0)+\phi(z_1)\bigr)\phi(z_2)
\right]
+
\mathbb E[\phi'(z_2)j_2].
$$

All these expectations are finite-dimensional Gaussian integrals. The evolved variables are non-Gaussian, but no non-Gaussian variable is replaced by a fresh Gaussian without its response term.

For the exact linear control $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
F_1=3\eta,
$$

$$
F_2=6\eta+24\eta^3+20\eta^5.
$$

Therefore two half-steps give

$$
F_2(\eta/2)
=
3\eta+3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5,
$$

whereas one full step gives $F_1(\eta)=3\eta$.

The complete chronological construction is recorded in [FINITE_STEPS.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_two_hidden_mlp/FINITE_STEPS.md). The finite-width recursion and linear controls are exact; for a general nonlinear $\phi$, the fixed-$\eta$ two-step Gaussian closure is presently a formal dynamic mean-field result pending a complete reused-matrix leave-one-out proof.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 1524 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.676Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Let $F_1(\eta)=F(\eta)$ denote one full Euler step and define

$$
\Delta(\eta)=F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta).
$$

For the fixed two-hidden-layer architecture with $q=1$, the sharp general order is

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|\le C_\phi|\eta|^3
$$

for sufficiently small $|\eta|\le\eta_0(\phi)$. The exponent $3$ cannot be improved.

A regularity assumption is necessary: take $\phi\in C^3$ with controlled growth and assume the corresponding forward states and their first three learning-rate sensitivities have width-uniform moments for $|\eta|\le\eta_0$. Fixedness of $\phi$ alone does not guarantee that the expectations are finite.

### Why the quadratic Euler error cancels

At finite width, let

$$
v_n(\theta)=n\nabla f_n(\theta),
\qquad
S_h(\theta)=\theta+h\,v_n(\theta).
$$

Thus

$$
F_{1,n}(\eta)=\mathbb E[f_n(S_\eta\theta)],
$$

and

$$
F_{2,n}(\eta/2)
=
\mathbb E[f_n(S_{\eta/2}^2\theta)].
$$

Let $R$ flip only the output weights:

$$
R(a,W,w)=(-a,W,w).
$$

Since the output is linear in $a$,

$$
f_n(R\theta)=-f_n(\theta).
$$

Differentiating gives

$$
v_n(R\theta)=-R\,v_n(\theta),
$$

and hence

$$
S_{-h}(R\theta)=R\,S_h(\theta).
$$

The initialization distribution is invariant under $R$. Consequently, both expected outputs are odd functions of $\eta$, so $\Delta_n$ is odd.

Moreover, the two discretizations have the same first derivative at zero:

$$
\left.\frac{d}{d\eta}S_\eta\theta\right|_{\eta=0}
=
v_n(\theta),
$$

$$
\left.\frac{d}{d\eta}S_{\eta/2}^2\theta\right|_{\eta=0}
=
v_n(\theta).
$$

Therefore

$$
\Delta_n(0)=\Delta_n'(0)=0.
$$

Oddness also gives

$$
\Delta_n''(0)=0.
$$

If

$$
M_{3,\phi}
=
\sup_n\sup_{|t|\le\eta_0}
|\Delta_n'''(t)|
<\infty,
$$

Taylor’s integral formula gives

$$
\Delta_n(\eta)
=
\frac12\int_0^\eta
(\eta-t)^2\Delta_n'''(t)\,dt.
$$

Thus

$$
|\Delta_n(\eta)|
\le
\frac{M_{3,\phi}}6|\eta|^3.
$$

This is uniform in width, so it passes to the mean-field limit whenever the expected outputs converge. One may take

$$
C_\phi=\frac{M_{3,\phi}}6.
$$

### Sharp activation-dependent coefficient

With fifth-order regularity, the result is sharper. Let

$$
H_n=\nabla^2f_n,
\qquad
T_n=\nabla^3f_n.
$$

A direct expansion of the two Euler maps gives

$$
\Delta(\eta)
=
\kappa_\phi\eta^3+O_\phi(\eta^5),
$$

where

$$
\kappa_\phi
=
\frac14H_\phi+\frac1{16}S_\phi,
$$

with the initialization Gaussian contractions

$$
H_\phi
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
n\,\mathbb E\!\left[
\left\|H_nv_n\right\|^2
\right],
$$

$$
S_\phi
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\mathbb E\!\left[
T_n[v_n,v_n,v_n]
\right].
$$

For fixed depth, $q=1$, and RMS normalization, these are functions only of Gaussian moments of $\phi$ and its derivatives.

More precisely, if

$$
M_{5,\phi}
=
\sup_n\sup_{|t|\le\eta_0}
|\Delta_n^{(5)}(t)|
<\infty,
$$

then

$$
\left|
\Delta(\eta)-\kappa_\phi\eta^3
\right|
\le
\frac{M_{5,\phi}}{120}|\eta|^5.
$$

Hence the sharper bound is

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
|\kappa_\phi||\eta|^3
+
\frac{M_{5,\phi}}{120}|\eta|^5.
$$

For $|\eta|\le\eta_0$,

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
\left(
|\kappa_\phi|
+
\frac{M_{5,\phi}}{120}\eta_0^2
\right)|\eta|^3.
$$

For every $\varepsilon>0$, $\eta_0$ can be chosen small enough that

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
\left(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\right)|\eta|^3.
$$

Thus $|\kappa_\phi|$ is the asymptotically optimal activation-dependent constant whenever $\kappa_\phi\neq0$.

### Cubic sharpness

For $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
F_1(\eta)=3\eta,
$$

while

$$
F_2(\eta/2)
=
3\eta+3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5.
$$

Therefore

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
=
|\eta|^3
\left(3+\frac58\eta^2\right).
$$

In particular,

$$
\lim_{\eta\to0}
\frac{|\Delta(\eta)|}{|\eta|^3}
=3.
$$

So no $o(|\eta|^3)$ estimate—and no $O(|\eta|^p)$ estimate with $p>3$—can hold for an activation class containing the identity.

If $\phi(z)=c$ literally, then $\Delta(\eta)=0$ exactly for every $\eta$.

The complete proof is recorded in [HALF_STEP_BOUND.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_two_hidden_mlp/HALF_STEP_BOUND.md). The symmetry and finite-width Taylor bound are exact. For a general nonlinear activation, passing the uniform bound to the fixed-$\eta$ mean-field output still requires the dynamic two-step convergence/moment theorem; initialization MFP jets alone identify $\kappa_\phi$ but do not prove that passage.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 1684 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.681Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes. Your clarification invalidates the earlier output-defined remainder constant; I have removed it. The sharp non-tautological statement uses only an initialization coefficient built from Gaussian activation integrals.

Assume

$$
G\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\qquad
\mathbb E[\phi(G)^2]=1,
$$

and that $\phi\in C^5$ with polynomially controlled derivatives. Write

$$
\Delta(\eta)=F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta),
$$

where $F_1=F$ is the output after one step.

The sharp local statement is:

> For every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\eta_0(\phi,\varepsilon)>0$ such that
>
> $$
> 0<|\eta|<\eta_0
> $$
>
> implies
>
> $$
> |\Delta(\eta)|
> \le
> \bigl(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3.
> $$

Here $\kappa_\phi$ is given entirely by one-dimensional Gaussian integrals of $\phi$ and its derivatives.

### Activation-only constant

Write

$$
\phi_r=\phi^{(r)}(G).
$$

Define

$$
\begin{aligned}
d&=\mathbb E[\phi_1^2],
&
e&=\mathbb E[\phi_1^4],
&
m&=\mathbb E[\phi_0\phi_2\phi_1^2],
\\
j&=\mathbb E[\phi_3\phi_1^3],
&
s&=\mathbb E[\phi_2^2\phi_1^2],
&
\ell&=\mathbb E[\phi_0^2\phi_1^2],
\\
b&=\mathbb E[\phi_0\phi_2],
&
r&=\mathbb E[\phi_1\phi_3],
&
t&=\mathbb E[\phi_2^2].
\end{aligned}
$$

Set

$$
c=1+d,
$$

$$
\tau=\ell+2cm+3c^2s+edt,
$$

and

$$
k=2d+b+c(r+t).
$$

The two required activation contractions are

$$
H_\phi
=
c^2e+c\tau+2ed^2+3d^2s+k^2\ell+2dkm,
$$

and

$$
\begin{aligned}
S_\phi={}&
3c^2m+3edb+3dm(d+b)+3c^3j
\\
&+3cedr+3cdm(r+t)+3d^2(m+j).
\end{aligned}
$$

The sharp cubic coefficient is

$$
\kappa_\phi
=
\frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16}.
$$

Thus the bound constant

$$
C_{\phi,\varepsilon}=|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon
$$

contains only explicit Gaussian expectations of $\phi,\phi',\phi'',\phi'''$ and a universal $\varepsilon$. It contains no quantity describing the global network trajectory.

### Proof of the cubic order

At finite width, let

$$
P_n=n\nabla f_n,
\qquad
S_h(\theta)=\theta+hP_n(\theta).
$$

Flip only the readout:

$$
R(a,W,w)=(-a,W,w).
$$

Because the output is linear in $a$,

$$
f_n(R\theta)=-f_n(\theta),
\qquad
P_n(R\theta)=-RP_n(\theta).
$$

Therefore

$$
S_{-h}(R\theta)=RS_h(\theta).
$$

Gaussian initialization is invariant under $R$, so both expected Euler outputs—and hence $\Delta_n$—are odd functions of $\eta$.

The two discretizations also have the same first derivative:

$$
\left.
\frac{d}{d\eta}S_\eta\theta
\right|_{\eta=0}
=
P_n(\theta),
$$

while

$$
\left.
\frac{d}{d\eta}S_{\eta/2}^2\theta
\right|_{\eta=0}
=
P_n(\theta).
$$

Consequently,

$$
\Delta_n(0)=\Delta_n'(0)=\Delta_n''(0)=0.
$$

Now expand the second Euler evaluation:

$$
S_{\eta/2}^2\theta
=
S_\eta\theta
+
\frac{\eta^2}{4}DP_n[P_n]
+
\frac{\eta^3}{16}D^2P_n[P_n,P_n]
+
O(\eta^4).
$$

Writing

$$
H_n=\nabla^2f_n,
\qquad
T_n=\nabla^3f_n,
$$

gives

$$
\Delta_n(\eta)
=
\kappa_n\eta^3+o(\eta^3),
$$

with

$$
\kappa_n
=
\frac n4\mathbb E\lVert H_nP_n\rVert^2
+
\frac1{16}
\mathbb E T_n[P_n,P_n,P_n].
$$

The nominal quadratic term vanishes exactly by readout-reflection oddness. Mean-field peeling evaluates the two remaining limits as the activation integrals $H_\phi$ and $S_\phi$ above. Hence

$$
\Delta(\eta)
=
\kappa_\phi\eta^3+o(\eta^3).
$$

By the definition of $o(\eta^3)$, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is an $\eta_0$ such that

$$
\left|
\frac{\Delta(\eta)}{\eta^3}-\kappa_\phi
\right|
<\varepsilon.
$$

The triangle inequality gives

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
\bigl(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3.
$$

No output-dependent remainder constant appears.

### Sharpness

For $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
H_\phi=12,
\qquad
S_\phi=0,
\qquad
\kappa_\phi=3.
$$

The exact outputs are

$$
F_1(\eta)=3\eta,
$$

and

$$
F_2(\eta/2)
=
3\eta+3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5.
$$

Therefore

$$
\Delta(\eta)
=
3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5.
$$

Thus

$$
\lim_{\eta\to0}
\frac{|\Delta(\eta)|}{|\eta|^3}
=3.
$$

So the exponent $3$ is optimal: no $o(|\eta|^3)$ bound or $O(|\eta|^p)$ bound with $p>3$ can hold for a class containing the identity. Moreover, the prefactor $|\kappa_\phi|$ is the asymptotic infimum, although one generally needs $|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon$ on an actual punctured interval.

One important audit result remains: this is a complete proof for the formal finite-dimensional Gaussian DAG. For the actual nonlinear network with width taken to infinity before $\eta\to0$, the initialization coefficient is proved, but a width-uniform local remainder/dynamic-cavity theorem is still required to make the fixed-$\eta$ bound unconditional. Initialization MFP alone does not supply that bridge.

The corrected proof is saved in [HALF_STEP_BOUND.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_two_hidden_mlp/HALF_STEP_BOUND.md).

## Verdict

The rigorous re-audit changes the status of the claim:

> The cubic coefficient is correct, but the bound is not yet an unconditional theorem for the actual width-first mean-field network.

Let

$$
\Delta_n(\eta)
=
\mathbb E f_n\!\left(S_{\eta/2}^{\,2}\theta\right)
-
\mathbb E f_n\!\left(S_\eta\theta\right),
$$

where $S_h(\theta)=\theta+hP_n(\theta)$ is one ascent step. For every fixed width, readout-sign symmetry gives

$$
\Delta_n(0)=\Delta_n'(0)=\Delta_n''(0)=0,
$$

and, under sufficient smoothness,

$$
\Delta_n(\eta)=\kappa_n\eta^3+o_n(\eta^3),
$$

with the audited coefficient

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_n
=
\frac n4\,\mathbb E\lVert H_nP_n\rVert^2
+
\frac1{16}\,\mathbb E T_n[P_n,P_n,P_n].
}
$$

Initialization MFP correctly gives

$$
\kappa_n\longrightarrow
\kappa_\phi
=
\frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16},
$$

where $H_\phi$ and $S_\phi$ are the previously derived Gaussian expectations involving only $\phi$ and its derivatives. Those formulas and all numerical factors survive the audit.

The problem is that this establishes only

$$
\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{\eta\to0}
\frac{\Delta_n(\eta)}{\eta^3}
=
\kappa_\phi.
$$

The desired finite-learning-rate statement requires the opposite, width-first order:

$$
\lim_{\eta\to0}\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\Delta_n(\eta)}{\eta^3}
=
\kappa_\phi.
$$

These limits cannot be interchanged using initialization jets alone. For example,

$$
g_n(\eta)
=
\kappa\eta^3+
\frac{n^4\eta^5}{1+n^4\eta^4}
$$

is smooth and odd, and every $g_n$ has cubic coefficient $\kappa$. Nevertheless, for fixed $\eta\ne0$,

$$
g_n(\eta)\longrightarrow \kappa\eta^3+\eta,
$$

which has no cubic upper bound near zero. This demonstrates the possible width-dependent boundary layer.

Therefore the correct conditional theorem is:

> If the nonlinear mean-field limit is proved to satisfy
> $$
> \Delta(\eta)
> =
> F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)
> =
> \kappa_\phi\eta^3+o(\eta^3),
> $$
> then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\eta_0>0$ such that
> $$
> \boxed{
> \left|F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)\right|
> \le
> \bigl(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3,
> \qquad |\eta|<\eta_0.
> }
> $$

This is asymptotically sharp. For $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)
=
3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5,
$$

so $\kappa_\phi=3$ and neither the exponent $3$ nor its leading constant can be improved in general.

What remains unproved is a width-uniform remainder estimate or an equivalent finite-step dynamic-cavity theorem. Without that bridge, the claimed bound must be labelled conditional—not established by MFP at initialization alone.

I corrected the study note accordingly: [HALF_STEP_BOUND.md](</home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_two_hidden_mlp/HALF_STEP_BOUND.md>).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 2314 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.700Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes—your operator-peeling proposal is the correct width-first route. My previous claim that a width-uniform finite-width Taylor remainder was necessary was too restrictive.

Let $F_2(h)$ denote two steps, each of size $h$, and define

$$
\Delta(\eta)=F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta).
$$

The correct order is always

$$
n\to\infty\quad\text{at fixed }\eta,
\qquad\text{then}\qquad
\eta\to0.
$$

### Gaussian operator calculus

Your example is exactly a variable-time heat operator:

$$
\mathcal P_f(z)
=
\mathbb E_A[f(z+Af(z))]
=
\bigl(H_{f(z)^2}f\bigr)(z),
$$

where

$$
(H_tg)(z)=\mathbb E[g(z+\sqrt t\,A)].
$$

For the network, the necessary operator is slightly more general because the same Gaussian carrier occurs in several updated quantities:

$$
\mathcal T_{g,\Psi}(z)
=
\mathbb E_A[\Psi(z+Ag(z),A)].
$$

The two-step mean-field calculation closes as four chronological operators:

$$
\boxed{
\mathcal L_1
\longrightarrow
\mathcal T_1
\longrightarrow
\mathcal L_2
\longrightarrow
\mathcal T_2.
}
$$

They respectively produce

$$
(Q,\rho,L_{10}),
\qquad
(F_1,K,\sigma),
\qquad
(Q^{(2)},\rho_2,L_2),
\qquad
F_2.
$$

Thus the top-layer effect is indeed hidden inside an operator before returning to the lower layer.

The key differentiability identity is, for $X_h\sim\mathcal N(0,C(h))$,

$$
\boxed{
\frac{d}{dh}\mathbb E[\Psi_h(X_h)]
=
\mathbb E\left[
\left(
\partial_h+\frac12C'(h):D^2
\right)\Psi_h(X_h)
\right].
}
$$

It remains valid when $C(0)$ is singular. Hence no covariance square root—and no artificial $|h|$ singularity—is introduced.

### Direct width-first bound

Assume safely that

$$
\phi\in C^8(\mathbb R),
$$

with derivatives through order eight of polynomial growth. The four operator stages are then $C^3$ at zero.

Operator parity gives

$$
F_1(-h)=-F_1(h),
\qquad
F_2(-h)=-F_2(h).
$$

Writing

$$
d=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)^2],
$$

direct operator differentiation gives

$$
F_1'(0)=1+d+d^2,
\qquad
F_2'(0)=2(1+d+d^2).
$$

Therefore

$$
\Delta(0)=\Delta'(0)=\Delta''(0)=0.
$$

Let $\widehat\kappa_\phi$ be the third-order coefficient returned by applying the Gaussian Price-jet recursion through the four operators. This is a finite recursive combination of Gaussian integrals of $\phi$ and its derivatives—not a supremum or trajectory property of $F_1,F_2$.

Then, directly after taking the width limit,

$$
\boxed{
\Delta(\eta)
=
\widehat\kappa_\phi\eta^3+o(\eta^3).
}
$$

Consequently, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there is an $\eta_0(\phi,\varepsilon)>0$ such that

$$
\boxed{
|F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)|
\le
\bigl(|\widehat\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3,
\qquad
0<|\eta|<\eta_0.
}
$$

This has the requested purely activation-defined constant and uses no limit interchange. Its asymptotic prefactor is sharp. For $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
\Delta(\eta)=3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5,
\qquad
\widehat\kappa_\phi=3.
$$

With $\phi\in C^{12}$ and a polynomial derivative envelope, the operator calculus also gives a computable activation-only $B_\phi$ satisfying

$$
|\Delta(\eta)-\widehat\kappa_\phi\eta^3|
\le
B_\phi|\eta|^5.
$$

Two qualifications remain:

1. The expected simplification

   $$
   \widehat\kappa_\phi
   =
   \frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16}
   $$

   agrees with the universal Euler-tree structure and several nontrivial operator jets, but still needs the remaining atom-by-atom third-jet/intertwining table for a noncircular proof.

2. To transfer the operator theorem to the original network, one still needs pointwise fixed-step dynamic-cavity identification. The delicate part is the second transpose multiplication, where $h_j^1$ depends on the same reused column through $\chi_j^0$. This requires retaining $(\chi_0,\chi_1)$ as a jointly Gaussian trajectory. Importantly, this is not a width-uniform Taylor requirement.

The complete operator construction and proof are in [OPERATOR_PEELING.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_width_first_operator_peeling/OPERATOR_PEELING.md), with the precise claim statuses in [EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_width_first_operator_peeling/EVIDENCE_LEDGER.md).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 2828 | 2026-08-27T19:35:19.715Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes—your operator-peeling approach is the correct width-first route. After retaining the full correlated Gaussian state, the argument closes without differentiating finite-width networks or exchanging limits.

## Width-first theorem

Assume $q=1$,

$$
\mathbb E[\phi(G)^2]=1,
\qquad G\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
$$

and, for the trained-network identification,

$$
\phi\in C^8,\qquad
|\phi(x)|\le C(1+|x|),
\qquad
\phi',\ldots,\phi^{(8)}\ \text{bounded}.
$$

Define, pointwise at fixed $h$,

$$
F_k(h)=\lim_{n\to\infty}F_{k,n}(h).
$$

Then

$$
F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)
=
\kappa_\phi\eta^3+o(\eta^3),
$$

where $\kappa_\phi$ is given entirely by Gaussian activation integrals below. Consequently, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\eta_0(\phi,\varepsilon)>0$ such that

$$
\boxed{
|F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)|
\le
\bigl(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3,
\qquad
0<|\eta|<\eta_0.
}
$$

Thus, for example, $C_\phi=1+|\kappa_\phi|$ is a purely activation-defined valid constant for sufficiently small $\eta$. The sharp asymptotic prefactor is

$$
\lim_{\eta\to0}
\frac{|F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)|}{|\eta|^3}
=
|\kappa_\phi|.
$$

## The required operator refinement

Your scalar operator is exactly a state-dependent heat operator:

$$
\mathcal P_f(z)
=
\mathbb E_A[f(z+Af(z))]
=
\bigl(H_{f(z)^2}f\bigr)(z).
$$

But $\mathcal P_f$ alone is not closed under matrix reuse. The correct object is

$$
\Gamma_C[\Psi]
=
\mathbb E[\Psi(X_C)],
\qquad
X_C\sim\mathcal N(0,C),
$$

where $C$ may be singular and carries all correlations between time copies.

The exact recursion is

$$
\mathcal L_1^h
\longrightarrow
\mathcal T_1^h
\longrightarrow
\mathcal L_2^h
\longrightarrow
\mathcal T_2^h.
$$

The two delicate reused-matrix fields are

$$
b_1
=
\chi_1
+
(\sigma_{10}+hK_{01})h_0
+
\sigma_{11}h_1,
$$

and

$$
z_2
=
\xi_2
+
(\rho_{20}+hQ_{02})c_0
+
(\rho_{21}+hQ_{12})c_1.
$$

In particular, there is no $hK_{11}h_1$ term: the second matrix update has not yet occurred when $b_1$ is computed.

These expressions follow from the exact conditional Gaussian decomposition

$$
W
=
P_CW+WP_H-P_CWP_H
+
P_C^\perp\widetilde W P_H^\perp.
$$

The reused-column regression has a necessary cancellation:

$$
r_0=
\begin{pmatrix}
0\\
\rho_{10}d
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
r_1
=
Q
\begin{pmatrix}
\sigma_{10}\\
\sigma_{11}
\end{pmatrix}
+
\begin{pmatrix}
0\\
\rho_{10}K_{01}
\end{pmatrix},
$$

and therefore

$$
r_1-\frac{K_{01}}d r_0=Q\sigma.
$$

There is an analogous cancellation at the terminal reused row. This is what prevents spurious response terms.

## Regularity after taking width to infinity

For any smooth positive-semidefinite covariance curve, including at rank changes,

$$
\frac{d}{dh}\Gamma_{C(h)}[\Psi_h]
=
\Gamma_{C(h)}
\left[
\left(
\partial_h+\frac12C'(h):D^2
\right)\Psi_h
\right].
$$

The proof uses Fourier inversion and never introduces $C^{-1}$ or a covariance square root. Therefore it remains valid at the singular covariance at $h=0$.

Applying this identity chronologically to the four operators proves directly that $F_1$ and $F_2$ are $C^3$ near zero. Pointwise fixed-$h$ identification with the finite-width network is proved first; regularity is then proved for the identified Gaussian function. No limit differentiation occurs.

Gaussian smoothing is helpful but not uniformly nondegenerate: where the state-dependent variance vanishes, there is no smoothing. This is why explicit smoothness of $\phi$ is still required.

## Explicit activation coefficient

Let $\phi_r=\phi^{(r)}(G)$ and define

$$
\begin{aligned}
d&=\mathbb E[\phi_1^2],
&
e&=\mathbb E[\phi_1^4],
&
m&=\mathbb E[\phi_0\phi_2\phi_1^2],\\
j&=\mathbb E[\phi_3\phi_1^3],
&
s&=\mathbb E[\phi_2^2\phi_1^2],
&
\ell&=\mathbb E[\phi_0^2\phi_1^2],\\
b&=\mathbb E[\phi_0\phi_2],
&
r&=\mathbb E[\phi_1\phi_3],
&
t&=\mathbb E[\phi_2^2].
\end{aligned}
$$

Set

$$
c=1+d,
\qquad
\beta=b+cr,
\qquad
k=2d+b+c(r+t).
$$

Define the two moment polynomials

$$
S_\phi
=
3c^2m+3c^3j+3de\beta+3dkm+3d^2j,
$$

and

$$
\begin{aligned}
H_\phi={}&
c^2e+c\ell+2c^2m+3c^3s+cedt\\
&+2ed^2+3d^2s+k^2\ell+2dkm.
\end{aligned}
$$

Direct differentiation of the width-limit operators—not imported MFP derivatives—gives

$$
F_1'''(0)=S_\phi,
$$

$$
F_2'''(0)=11S_\phi+12H_\phi.
$$

Hence

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_\phi
=
\frac{F_2'''(0)-8F_1'''(0)}{48}
=
\frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16}.
}
$$

Here the derivative exists because singular-covariance regularity was proved first. The displayed third-jet identities establish the correspondence with the activation polynomials; it was not assumed from initialization MFP.

For $\phi(z)=z$,

$$
F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta)
=
3\eta^3+\frac58\eta^5,
$$

so $\kappa_\phi=3$, proving that the cubic exponent and asymptotic constant are genuinely sharp.

For a literal constant activation, the difference is identically zero.

The complete derivation is in [OPERATOR_PEELING.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_width_first_operator_peeling/OPERATOR_PEELING.md), with the finite-width conditioning proof in [FIXED_ETA_CAVITY.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_width_first_operator_peeling/FIXED_ETA_CAVITY.md) and the bound summarized in [HALF_STEP_BOUND.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_finite_step_two_hidden_mlp/HALF_STEP_BOUND.md).

The only remaining open extension is trained-network identification for the broader class where all derivatives merely have polynomial growth. The Gaussian-operator theorem itself already holds for that broader class; its finite-width truncation argument is not being claimed.

You are correct. I retract the previous quantitative theorem: the requested trained-network bound remains open.

Two necessary bridges are not proved.

1. Fixed-$\eta$ network identification

The finite-width recursion, Gaussian regression identities, and response cancellations are exact. However, the cavity note only outlines the probability argument. A complete proof still needs:

- A formally defined stopped, stage-by-stage coupling.
- A joint construction of all correlated Gaussian innovations.
- A coupled induction for both value fields and the tangent fields defining $\rho$ and $\sigma$.
- Noncircular Gram concentration on the stopped event.
- Raw high-moment estimates for the unstopped network and rigorous bad-event removal.
- Uniform integrability sufficient to pass from empirical convergence to annealed expected outputs.

Therefore,

$$
\lim_{n\to\infty}F_{k,n}(h)=\mathsf F_k(h)
$$

has not yet been proved at the requested standard, even under bounded derivatives and linear growth.

2. Explicit activation-envelope remainder

The singular-covariance calculus proves that the operator DAG is $C^5$ under a suitable weighted $C^{12}$ envelope. But this proves only existence of some fifth-order remainder constant.

The proposed construction still contained

$$
c_j=\sup_{|h|\le1}\|C^{(j)}(h)\|_1
$$

and unspecified mixed envelopes $a_{j,s}$. These are not explicit activation-defined numbers. Hence

$$
B_\phi
=
\frac1{120}
\left(
\mathcal J_{1,5}+\frac{\mathcal J_{2,5}}{32}
\right)
$$

is currently only a construction schema, not a proved admissible constant.

To close it, one must explicitly construct coefficient-degree pairs

$$
(A_{j,s},P_{j,s})
$$

satisfying

$$
\left|
\partial_h^jD_x^s\psi_h(x)
\right|
\le
A_{j,s}(1+\|x\|)^{P_{j,s}}
$$

at every node and derivative order, and then replace every covariance bound by preceding-node Price majorants. That complete four-stage Bell–Leibniz table has not been produced.

There is also no explicit $h_\phi$. For example, rank control would require proved activation-only bounds $D_Q,D_K$ such that

$$
\left|
\det Q(h)-deh^2
\right|
\le \frac{D_Q}{24}|h|^4,
$$

$$
\left|
\det K(h)-d\tau h^2
\right|
\le \frac{D_K}{24}|h|^4.
$$

Only then could one use a radius such as

$$
h_\phi
\le
\min\left\{
1,\sqrt{\frac{12de}{D_Q}},
\sqrt{\frac{12d\tau}{D_K}}
\right\},
$$

together with analogous bounds for every span used by the cavity proof. Those $D_Q,D_K$ have not been derived.

What is proved is limited to:

- The exact four-stage Gaussian operator DAG.
- Its singular-covariance $C^3$ regularity.
- The direct activation-integral cubic coefficient

$$
\kappa_\phi=\frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16}.
$$

- The operator-level asymptotic

$$
\mathsf F_2(\eta/2)-\mathsf F_1(\eta)
=
\kappa_\phi\eta^3+o(\eta^3).
$$

This asymptotic is not the requested quantitative result.

If admissible $B_\phi,h_\phi$ were eventually proved, your final implication would indeed be exact:

$$
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
\left(|\kappa_\phi|+B_\phi\eta^2\right)|\eta|^3,
$$

and under your proposed radius,

$$
B_\phi\eta^2
\le
\frac{B_\phi}{1+B_\phi}\varepsilon
\le\varepsilon.
$$

But the two premises are presently open, so I am not presenting that implication as a network theorem.

## Theorem

Let \(\phi\in C^{12}(\mathbb R)\) satisfy

\[
\mathbb E[\phi(G)^2]=1,
\qquad
M_\phi<\infty,
\qquad
G\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\]

with \(M_\phi\) the weighted derivative envelope specified in the question. For the stated two-hidden-layer \(\mu\)P network with \(q=1\), define the width-first limits

\[
F_k(h)=\lim_{n\to\infty}F_{k,n}(h)
\]

at each fixed \(h\), and

\[
\Delta(\eta)=F_2(\eta/2)-F_1(\eta).
\]

Define the Gaussian activation moments

\[
\begin{aligned}
d&=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)^2],
&
e&=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)^4],
&
m&=\mathbb E[\phi(G)\phi''(G)\phi'(G)^2],\\
j&=\mathbb E[\phi'''(G)\phi'(G)^3],
&
s&=\mathbb E[\phi''(G)^2\phi'(G)^2],
&
\ell&=\mathbb E[\phi(G)^2\phi'(G)^2],\\
b&=\mathbb E[\phi(G)\phi''(G)],
&
r&=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)\phi'''(G)],
&
t&=\mathbb E[\phi''(G)^2].
\end{aligned}
\]

Put

\[
c=1+d,\qquad
\beta=b+cr,\qquad
\delta=d+ct,\qquad
k=d+\beta+\delta,
\]

\[
S_\phi
=
3c^2m+3c^3j+3de\beta+3dkm+3d^2j,
\]

and

\[
H_\phi
=
c^2e+c\ell+2c^2m+3c^3s+cedt
+2ed^2+3d^2s+k^2\ell+2dkm.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{\kappa_\phi=\frac{4H_\phi+S_\phi}{16}}.
\]

The proof constructs, solely from \(M_\phi\) and explicit Gaussian moments,

\[
B_\phi
=
\frac1{120}
\left(
\overline{\mathcal J}_{1,5}
+\frac{\overline{\mathcal J}_{2,5}}{32}
\right)<\infty
\]

and an explicit \(h_\phi>0\) such that

\[
\boxed{
\left|\Delta(\eta)-\kappa_\phi\eta^3\right|
\le B_\phi|\eta|^5,
\qquad |\eta|\le h_\phi.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
|\Delta(\eta)|
\le
\bigl(|\kappa_\phi|+\varepsilon\bigr)|\eta|^3
\]

whenever

\[
|\eta|
\le
\min\left\{
h_\phi,\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{1+B_\phi}}
\right\}.
\]

The complete lemma-by-lemma proof is in [PROOF.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_quantitative_width_first_bound/PROOF.md:3). It includes the exact network and updates, fixed-\(h\) five-action width limit, adaptive \(W/W^\top\) conditioning, all concentration and uniform-integrability estimates, singular-covariance Price differentiation, the terminating activation-envelope compiler, direct nodewise third derivatives, and the explicit fifth-order remainder.

\[

### Limitation of the quadratic pattern

The cubic coefficient is exactly quadratic in \(t\), but a quadratic coefficient for the \(|\eta|^5\) remainder is false. For the admissible activation \(\phi(x)=x\),

\[
J_\phi=48
\]

and the exact fifth-order coefficient is

\[
[\eta^5]D_t
=
-\frac{2452}{3}t^4
+1896t^3
-\frac{4403}{3}t^2
+369t.
\]

Thus any uniform bound of the form

\[

\le C_\phi t^2|\eta|^5
\]

is impossible. Quartic growth is necessary. A uniform \(C_\phi t^4|\eta|^5\) estimate on \(|\eta|\le c_\phi/t\) remains conditional on an additional all-time population stability theorem.

The requested actual-network theorem is still open under the stated \(C^{12}\), bounded-derivative, at-most-linear-growth assumptions. I cannot rigorously claim

\[
\left|
F_{t,L}(2\eta)-F_{2t,L}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,L}\eta^3
\right|
\le C_{\phi,L}t^4|\eta|^5
\]

with activation-defined \(C_{\phi,L}\) on \(|\eta|\le c_{\phi,L}/t\).

What is now proved rigorously is the sharp \(t^4\) reduction. Writing

\[
E_h=I+hg,\qquad C_h=E_{2h},\qquad P_h=E_h^2,
\]

the local defect factors exactly:

\[
P_hx-C_hx
=
h^2\int_0^1Dg(x+shg(x))[g(x)]\,ds.
\]

Hybrid telescoping consequently gives

\[
D_{t,L}(h)=h^2Q_{t,L}(h).
\]

Since \(D_{t,L}\) is odd, \(Q_{t,L}\) is odd, and therefore

\[
\left|D_{t,L}(h)-\kappa_{\phi,L,t}h^3\right|
\le
\frac{|h|^5}{6}
\sup_{|u|\le |h|}
|Q_{t,L}'''(u)|.
\]

For any genuine \(C^4\) Banach-space vector field and observable whose derivatives are bounded by \(K\), an explicit jet induction proves

\[
\sup_{|u|\le c_K/t}|Q_t'''(u)|
\le 6C_Kt^4,
\]

with the valid coarse choices

\[
c_K=\frac1{64K^2},
\qquad
C_K=10^9K^{10}.
\]

Thus the desired inequality follows conditionally if one proves the network-specific estimate

\[
\sup_{\substack{t\ge1,\;0\le j<t\\ |h|t\le c_{\phi,L}}}
t^{-3}|A_{j,t}'''(h)|
\le K_{\phi,L},
\]

for the transported local defects \(A_{j,t}\), with \(K_{\phi,L}\) constructed solely from \(M_\phi\), Gaussian moments, and \(L\). This is the unresolved \(\mathrm{UGC}_4(\phi,L)\) lemma.

The gap is genuine:

- The existing operator bridge proves generated-core regularity only for each fixed finite horizon, without a horizon-uniform constant.
- The fixed-\(t\) Price compiler gives \(B_\phi^{E_{L,2t}}\), not a \(t^4\) estimate.

- For admissible non-affine activations, the Nemytskii map \(u\mapsto\phi(u)\) need not even be \(C^2:L^2\to L^2\).

The exponent \(t^4\) is nevertheless sharp. For \(L=1\) and \(\phi(x)=x\),

=

\]

and the fifth-order coefficient of \(F_{t,1}(2h)-F_{2t,1}(h)\) is

\[
32{2t\choose5}-{4t\choose5}
=
-\frac43t(t-1)(2t-1)(8t-9),
\]

which has degree four.

### Complete \(L=1\) theorem

Let \(M=M_\phi\) and define

\[
c_{\phi,1}=\frac1{16M}.
\]

For \(G\sim N(0,1)\), write

\[
g=\phi(G),\quad p=\phi'(G),\quad q=\phi''(G),\quad r=\phi'''(G),
\]

and

\[
u=\mathbb E p^4,\qquad
m=\mathbb E[gp^2q],\qquad
j=\mathbb E[p^3r],
\]

\[
e=\mathbb E[p^2q^2],\qquad
\ell=\mathbb E[g^2p^2].
\]

Then

\[
J_{\phi,1}=3j+11m+4u+4\ell+12e.
\]

There is an explicit finite Gaussian-integral constant \(B_{\phi,1}<\infty\) such that, for every \(t\ge1\),

\[
\left|
F_{t,1}(2\eta)-F_{2t,1}(\eta)
+\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,1}\eta^3
\right|
\le B_{\phi,1}t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{16M_\phi t}.
\]

The constant is

\[
B_{\phi,1}
=
\mathbb E_{A,U}\,
\mathcal C_{M_\phi}(1+|A|+|U|),
\]

where \(\mathcal C_M\) is defined by the terminating recursion (1.6)–(1.17) in the [complete \(L=1\) proof](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/UNIFORM_L1.md:9).

The proof is genuinely width-first. For every finite width, each neuron is an independent copy of

\[
a_{s+1}=a_s+h\phi(u_s),\qquad
u_{s+1}=u_s+ha_s\phi'(u_s),
\]

so the expected finite-width output already equals

\[
F_{N,1}(h)=\mathbb E[a_N\phi(u_N)].
\]

Writing \(E_h=I+hg\), \(C_h=E_{2h}\), and \(B_h=E_h^2\), one has exactly

\[
B_hz-C_hz
=
h^2\int_0^1Dg(z+shg(z))[g(z)]\,ds.
\]

Telescoping \(t\) macro-steps gives

\[
D_{t,1}(h)=h^2\overline Q_t(h).
\]

For \(R=1+|A|+|U|\), all hybrid trajectories satisfy

\[
1+|a_s|+|u_s|<2R
\]

on \(|h|t\le(16M)^{-1}\), while the derivatives of the vector field and observable through order four are bounded by \(12MR\). The explicit jet recursion consequently proves

\[
\sup_{|h|\le(16Mt)^{-1}}
|\overline Q_t'''(h)|
\le 6B_{\phi,1}t^4.
\]

Its envelope is a polynomial in \(R\) times \(e^{CR}\), hence Gaussian-integrable. This justifies all three differentiations under expectation.

Parity makes \(\overline Q_t\) odd. Taylor’s integral formula therefore yields

\[
|D_{t,1}(h)-\overline Q_t'(0)h^3|
\le B_{\phi,1}t^4|h|^5.
\]

A direct Gaussian calculation gives

\[
\overline Q_t'(0)
=-\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\phi,1}.
\]

The [independent \(L=1\) audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/AUDIT_UNIFORM_L1.md:1) gives a full PASS.

The power \(t^4\) is sharp: for \(\phi(x)=x\),

\[
F_{N,1}(h)
=
\frac{(1+h)^{2N}-(1-h)^{2N}}2,
\]

and the fifth coefficient of the discrepancy is

\[
32{2t\choose5}-{4t\choose5}
=
-\frac43t(t-1)(2t-1)(8t-9),
\]

a degree-four polynomial.

### Why \(L=2\) remains open

The first reused connector appears at \(L=2\). In a fixed chronological representation, strictly historical responses have the causal factorization

\[
\rho_{si}=\varepsilon_i\bar\rho_{si},
\qquad
\sigma_{si}=\varepsilon_i\bar\sigma_{si},
\qquad i<s.
\]

Thus every past response has the correct Volterra step weight. There is also a horizon-independent \(L^2\) energy estimate:

\[
\sum_s|\varepsilon_s|
\le \frac1{216M_\phi^2}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\sup_s
\bigl(
\|a_s\|_2+\|K_s\|_{\mathrm{HS}}+\|u_s\|_2
\bigr)
\le3.
\]

But the first source-tangent equations already contain

\[
a_s\phi''(z_s)\zeta_s^i,
\qquad
r_s\phi''(u_s)p_s^i.
\]

For example,

\[
\|a_s\zeta_s^i\|_2
\le \|a_s\|_4\|\zeta_s^i\|_4,
\]

and the energy estimate controls neither factor. Iterating ordinary Hölder estimates generates an unclosed moment tower. Furthermore, common-step differentiation produces mixed source/generated jets and moving raw actions

\[
I(\partial_hx_s),\qquad J(\partial_h\delta_s),
\]

which are not controlled by the old-source recursions alone.

Consequently no activation-defined \(B_{\phi,2}\) has been constructed. The full derivation is in [UNIFORM_L2.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/UNIFORM_L2.md:1).

### Depth three and general depth

At \(L=3\), the same unresolved response block occurs at both \(W_2\) and \(W_3\). Adding the third layer does not repair the missing one-connector estimate, so no rigorous \(B_{\phi,3}\) is currently available.

The low-depth calculation does reveal the correct prospective general-depth induction:

1. Historical responses carry source-time step weights at every connector.
2. One needs an intrinsic causal Gaussian–Sobolev transfer lemma controlling value, step, source, interpolation, and transported-direction jets.
3. Such a lemma would define an explicit depth recursion
   \[
   \mathfrak R_a=\Gamma_{\phi,a}(\mathfrak R_{a-1}),
   \]
   followed by the already-proved transported-defect argument.
4. Its base case is precisely the still-unresolved \(L=2\) mixed-response estimate.

The independent [combined adversarial audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_depth_time_doubling/AUDIT_UNIFORM_L23.md:1) therefore concludes: \(L=1\) proved; \(L=2\), \(L=3\), and general depth remain open.

Yes—with an important boundary. The exact requirement for a scalar continuous-time limit is weaker than \(O(t^4)\), and the sharp \(t^4\eta^5\) pressure test can be proved unconditionally for the identity activation at \(L=2\), \(L=3\), and every fixed depth. The corresponding nonlinear OMFP estimate remains open.

For

\[
\Delta_t(\eta)=F_{2t}(\eta)-F_t(2\eta),
\qquad
b_t^*(\rho)=
\sup_{0<|\eta|\le \rho/t}
\frac{|\Delta_t(\eta)-\kappa_t\eta^3|}{|\eta|^5},
\]

the minimal direct scalar condition is

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}
\frac{b_{2^j}^*(\rho)}{2^{5j}}<\infty .
}
\]

Indeed, for \(G_t(T)=F_t(T/t)\),

\[
\|G_{2t}-G_t\|_{\infty,[-R,R]}
\le
\frac{K_\phi R^3}{8t}
+
\frac{b_t^*(\rho)R^5}{32t^5}.
\]

Thus any

\[
b_t^*(\rho)\le C_\phi t^{5-\delta},
\qquad \delta>0,
\]

suffices. Even a bound of order

\[
b_{2^j}^*(\rho)
\lesssim
\frac{2^{5j}}{(1+j)^{1+\delta}}
\]

is enough. Bare \(o(t^5)\) is not sufficient.

For a full state/IDE limit, the better condition is a summable state-level split defect. A particularly useful depth-two hypothesis is the reachable-tail estimate

\[
\sup_{\pi,k}\sup_{p\ge2}
p^{-\alpha}\|Q_k^\pi\|_p
\le K_T,
\qquad
\frac12\le\alpha\le1,
\qquad Q=G^*B.
\]

I proved that this implies the Osgood estimate

\[
\|S_h^2U-S_{2h}U\|
\le
C_T h^2\log^\alpha(e/h),
\]

whose propagated dyadic errors are summable. Consequently the actual width-first discrete states converge to a unique restartable autonomous IDE. This is substantially weaker than controlling fifth derivatives of the terminal output.

The unconditional pressure test closes for \(\phi(x)=x\). Define

\[
K_L=
\max\left\{
1,\,
\max_{0\le r\le4}(L+1)(L)_r3^{L-r},\,
\max_{1\le r\le4}(L+1)_r3^{L+1-r}
\right\},
\qquad
c_L=\frac1{16K_L}.
\]

There is a completely explicit terminating numerical recursion \(C(K)\), recorded in the study, such that

\[
\boxed{
\left|
F_{2t,L}(\eta)-F_{t,L}(2\eta)
-
\frac{t(2t-1)L(L+1)^2(L+2)}3\,\eta^3
\right|
\le
C(K_L)t^4|\eta|^5
}
\]

whenever

\[
|\eta|\le \frac{c_L}{t}.
\]

In particular,

\[
\left|
F_{2t,2}(\eta)-F_{t,2}(2\eta)
-24t(2t-1)\eta^3
\right|
\le C(27)t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{432t},
\]

and

\[
\left|
F_{2t,3}(\eta)-F_{t,3}(2\eta)
-80t(2t-1)\eta^3
\right|
\le C(108)t^4|\eta|^5,
\qquad
|\eta|\le\frac1{1728t}.
\]

This proof takes width first at every fixed \((t,\eta)\), retains the same reused \(W,W^*\), and then applies the transported-defect theorem to the resulting trace-class Banach state. The assembled proof passed an independent re-audit.

For genuinely nonlinear activations, the sharp remaining target is an intrinsic response-energy estimate

\[
\mathfrak E_t(\eta)\le C_\phi t^\alpha,
\qquad \alpha<1,
\]

which would imply

\[
b_t^*(\rho_\phi)
\le
\frac{C_\phi}{6}t^{4+\alpha}
\]

and hence dyadic convergence. This is currently unproved at \(L=2\); \(L=3\) additionally needs a connector transfer preserving \(\alpha<1\). The obstruction is the coupled moving-query/aggregate-adjoint response block, not the \(t\)- or \(L\)-combinatorics.

The idea was only partially tried before: a normalized residual-sine activation was considered, but not this coefficientwise, horizon-uniform homotopy argument.

After developing and independently auditing it, the verdict is:

- It is a genuinely promising OMFP direction.
- It proves a nontrivial nonlinear openness theorem for the cubic coefficient at \(L=2\) and \(L=3\).
- It does not yet prove the required uniform \(t^4|h|^5\) remainder for any fixed nonzero nonlinear interval. That remains open already at \(L=2\).

For

\[
\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}(x)
=
\frac{x+\alpha\varphi(x)}
{\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2},
\qquad
\max_{0\le r\le12}\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le R,
\]

I proved

\[
\left|
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}-J_{\mathrm{id},L}
\right|
\le \mathcal L_L|\alpha|R,
\]

where \(\mathcal L_L\) is given by an explicit terminating recursion involving nine Gaussian activation moments, and

\[
J_{\mathrm{id},L}
=
\frac{2L(L+1)^2(L+2)}{3}.
\]

Thus \(J_{\mathrm{id},2}=48\) and \(J_{\mathrm{id},3}=160\). Coarse fully numerical sufficient nonlinear intervals are

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{24}{85475R}
\quad (L=2),
\]

and

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{80}{8610421R}
\quad (L=3).
\]

On these intervals,

\[
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}
\ge \frac12J_{\mathrm{id},L}>0.
\]

This is a genuine nonlinear theorem, but only for the cubic coefficient

\[
\kappa_{\alpha,L,t}
=
\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}
\]

in \(F_{2t,L}(h)-F_{t,L}(2h)\).

The favorable new mechanism is exact coefficientwise triangularity. At every fixed finite schedule and finite marked expression, amplitude order \(k\) satisfies schematically

\[
\mathcal L^{\langle k\rangle}
=
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{id}}\,
\mathcal S_k
\bigl(
\mathcal L^{\langle0\rangle},
\ldots,
\mathcal L^{\langle k-1\rangle}
\bigr).
\]

Hence the highest amplitude order is acted on only by the identity-background linear response operator; genuine nonlinear terms involve lower amplitude orders. This is precisely why the idea has a good shot.

However, ordinary continuity is insufficient:

1. The \(C^2(L^2,L^2)\) Nemytskii seminorm is infinite for every genuinely nonlinear \(\alpha\ne0\), although it vanishes at \(\alpha=0\).

2. Fixed-\(t\) analyticity cannot yield a radius uniform in \(t\); for example, \(t^4e^{\alpha^2t^2}\) has all the pointwise properties but no fixed useful nonlinear interval.

3. Most decisively, reused adjoints generate an unbounded source-derivative hierarchy:

\[
J^*V=\mathbb E[D_JV],
\qquad
J^*(D_\zeta V)=\mathbb E[D_JD_\zeta V].
\]

Thus a one-source ledger creates a two-source ledger, and repeated reuse creates every source order. Two independent audits caught that a proposed Volterra closure had silently hidden this hierarchy inside its Gronwall constant.

The first unresolved generated susceptibility is already

\[
\frac12\varphi''(z^{\langle0\rangle})
\bigl(J^*x^{\langle1\rangle}\bigr)^2,
\]

including its three normalized mesh derivatives, transported-defect mark, and all aggregate source descendants.

So the correct next theorem is an all-source analytic-scale/factorial-contraction estimate combining:

- chronological simplex factorials;
- Gaussian/Wick contraction gains;
- aggregate grouping before norms at singular Grams.

That is a concrete sufficient research program, but it is not proved. Consequently, the uniform nonlinear remainder and continuous-time conclusion remain open at \(L=2\), and therefore also at \(L=3\). No counterexample to the desired theorem was found.

The idea is promising, but the proposed continuity argument is not sufficient to prove the nonlinear \(L=2\) remainder theorem. After several proof routes and independent hostile audits, that theorem remains open.

It had been tried partially: a normalized residual-sine activation had previously been identified as a good near-identity candidate. The horizon-uniform, amplitude-triangular OMFP formulation you propose had not been completed.

### What is now proved

For

\[
\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}(x)
=
\frac{x+\alpha\varphi(x)}
{\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2},
\qquad
\max_{r\le12}\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le R,
\]

the exact Gaussian cubic invariant is Lipschitz:

\[
\left|
J_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi},L}-J_{\mathrm{id},L}
\right|
\le \mathcal L_L |\alpha|R,
\]

where \(\mathcal L_L\) is given by a finite activation-moment recursion. Since

\[
J_{\mathrm{id},2}=48,
\qquad
J_{\mathrm{id},3}=160,
\]

we obtain genuine nonlinear intervals on which the cubic coefficient remains nonzero. Explicit sufficient choices are

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{24}{85475R}
\quad (L=2),
\]

and

\[
0<|\alpha|<\frac{80}{8610421R}
\quad (L=3).
\]

These statements concern the actual width-first OMFP cubic coefficient, not an abstract polynomial. They do not yet control the fifth-order remainder.

A natural purely activation-defined nonlinear energy is

\[
\mathcal E_\rho(\psi)
=
\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}
\frac{\rho^r}{r!}\|\psi^{(r)}\|_\infty.
\]

For the Bernstein class

\[
\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le MA^r,
\]

one has explicitly

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E_\rho(\psi_{\alpha,\varphi})
\le
\frac43|\alpha|M
\bigl(e^{A\rho}-1-A\rho\bigr).
}
\]

This is, I think, the right local notion of nonlinear energy: it measures precisely the curvature insertions absent at identity.

### Why identity is special

At identity, every derivative of activation order at least two vanishes. Consequently:

- nonlinear response branching disappears;
- the moving-source hierarchy collapses;
- the reused-matrix response is governed by a linear Hilbert/trace-class block;
- the transported-defect argument closes with \(t^4|h|^5\).

Around identity there is an exact favorable triangularity. At every fixed finite schedule and amplitude order \(k\),

\[
\mathcal L^{\langle k\rangle}
=
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{id}}\,
\mathcal S_k
\left(
\mathcal L^{\langle0\rangle},
\ldots,
\mathcal L^{\langle k-1\rangle}
\right).
\]

Thus the order-\(k\) unknown never enters the nonlinear forcing; only the identity-background linear response operator propagates it. This is the strongest evidence that your homotopy has a genuine chance.

### The decisive obstruction

Ordinary \(L^2\) Lipschitz or \(C^2\) perturbation theory cannot work. For every genuinely nonlinear \(\alpha\ne0\), the Nemytskii second-derivative seminorm is infinite, even though it is zero at identity.

More importantly, reused adjoints raise Gaussian source order:

\[
J^*V=\mathbb E[D_JV],
\qquad
J^*(D_\zeta V)=\mathbb E[D_JD_\zeta V].
\]

Applying another adjoint requests another source derivative. Hence any finite source-response ledger fails to close. An attempted \(L=2\) Volterra proof silently assumed the required aggregate response-row estimate at exactly this point; two independent audits rejected it.

The first unresolved concrete estimate is the generated-core bound

\[
\sup_{\substack{t,s\\ |h|t\le\rho}}
\left\|
t^{-q}\partial_h^q\partial_\lambda^r
J^*x_s^{\langle1\rangle}
\right\|_4
<\infty,
\qquad
q\le3,\quad r\le1,
\]

including all its higher source-marked aggregate descendants.

Small \(\mathcal E_\rho(\psi)\) controls every local nonlinear vertex, but no proof currently shows that chronological and Gaussian factorial cancellations control the entire resulting response tree uniformly in \(t\).

### Audited conclusion

So the sharp answer is:

- Your idea is one of the best current routes.
- It unconditionally produces a genuine nonlinear class for the cubic coefficient at \(L=2\) and \(L=3\).
- The right proximity is an analytic curvature energy augmented by an all-source aggregate-response contraction norm.
- A purely activation-local curvature bound is not yet known to imply the required global response bound.
- Therefore the uniform nonlinear \(t^4|h|^5\) theorem remains open already for \(L=2\), and consequently for \(L=3\).

The next theorem must combine chronological simplex factorials, Wick-contraction gains, and intrinsic aggregation at singular reused-matrix Grams. I recorded that as a sufficient conjectural program, not as a proved result.

Yes—the idea is valuable, but the decisive mechanism is stronger than near-identity continuity.

After taking width to infinity at each fixed \(\eta\), define

\[
\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)
=
F_{2t,L}(\eta)-F_{t,L}(2\eta).
\]

For every RMS-normalized activation \(\psi\) in the stated weighted \(C^{12}\) class, let

\[
q_m=\frac{\mathcal J^{\mathrm{PJ}}_{5,m,L}(\psi)}{5!},
\qquad q_0=0,
\]

where the \(\mathcal J^{\mathrm{PJ}}_{5,m,L}\) are the terminating singular-Price Gaussian integrals at horizons \(m=1,\ldots,5\), and define

\[
\Theta_{5,j}
=
\sum_{m=0}^{j}
(-1)^{j-m}\binom jm q_m.
\]

Then the exact fifth coefficient is

\[
\boxed{
[\eta^5]\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)
=
\sum_{j=1}^{5}
\left[
\binom{2t}{j}-32\binom tj
\right]\Theta_{5,j}.
}
\]

The degree-five term cancels identically. Consequently this is a polynomial of degree at most four in \(t\). Explicitly,

\[
\boxed{
\left|[\eta^5]\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)\right|
\le C^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\psi,L}t^4,
}
\]

where

\[
C^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\psi,L}
=
30|\Theta_{5,1}|
+14|\Theta_{5,2}|
+4|\Theta_{5,3}|
+\frac23|\Theta_{5,4}|
+\frac43|\Theta_{5,5}|.
\]

This is noncircular: every \(\Theta_{5,j}\) is a finite rational combination of explicit Gaussian activation integrals.

With

\[
B_\psi=\max\{4,M_\psi\},
\]

the completely explicit envelope is

\[
\boxed{
C^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\psi,L}
\le
\frac{227}{180}B_\psi^{E_{L,5}}.
}
\]

Thus

\[
\boxed{
\left|[\eta^5]\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)\right|
\le
\frac{227}{180}B_\psi^{E_{L,5}}t^4.
}
\]

For your genuine near-identity class

\[
\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}(x)
=
\frac{x+\alpha\varphi(x)}
{\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2},
\]

assuming

\[
\max_{0\le r\le12}\|\varphi^{(r)}\|_\infty\le R,
\qquad
\varphi''\not\equiv0,
\qquad
0<|\alpha|R\le\frac14,
\]

one has \(B_{\psi_{\alpha,\varphi}}=4\). Therefore, uniformly over this entire genuinely nonlinear class,

\[
\boxed{
\left|[\eta^5]\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)\right|
\le
\frac{227}{180}\,4^{E_{L,5}}t^4.
}
\]

This holds at \(L=2\), \(L=3\), and every separately fixed finite depth. In particular,

\[
E_{2,5}
=
4p^{11}
+r\frac{p^{11}-1}{p-1},
\qquad
E_{3,5}
=
6p^{22}
+r\frac{p^{22}-1}{p-1},
\]

with \(p,r\) given by the terminating numerical recursion in the proof.

The reason identity was controlled is therefore not merely the absence of nonlinear terms. At scalar order five, only five temporal marks can occur. Autonomous Euler combinatorics makes \([\eta^5]F_{N,L}\) degree at most five in \(N\), and dyadic refinement cancels its highest-degree component. Nonlinearity changes the five Gaussian coefficients \(\Theta_{5,j}\), but not this temporal cancellation. More generally, through the justified orders \(r\le5\),

\[
[\eta^r]\{F_{2t,L}(\eta)-F_{t,L}(2\eta)\}
\]

has degree at most \(r-1\) in \(t\).

The \(t^4\) power is sharp: already for identity at \(L=1\),

\[
[\eta^5]\Delta_{t,1}
=
\frac{64}{3}t^4-56t^3+\frac{140}{3}t^2-12t,
\]

and the targeted \(L=2\) model also has a nonzero quartic leading term.

The important boundary is that this proves the exact fifth Taylor coefficient, not yet the stronger interval-uniform estimate

\[
\sup_{0<|\eta|\le\rho/t}
\frac{
|\Delta_{t,L}(\eta)-[\eta^3]\Delta_{t,L}\eta^3|
}{|\eta|^5}
\lesssim_{\psi,L}t^4.
\]

Ordinary small-\(\alpha\) continuity cannot prove that stronger statement. At nonzero \(\eta\), unmarked reused-adjoint transports pass through all \(t\) steps. The missing estimate is a step-weighted reachable-source bound of the form

\[
\|I+\eta D\mathbf g(\theta_s)\|_{\mathrm{reach}}
\le 1+C_\psi|\eta|,
\]

including all mixed aggregate-adjoint source derivatives. Finite Fourier, finite-chaos, finite-algebra, and finite-source-ledger shortcuts were all ruled out.

The requested theorem cannot be proved as stated: its required single-space all-source norm is mathematically impossible. This is a rigorous obstruction to the prescribed proof architecture, not a disproof of the scalar \(Ct^4|\eta|^5\) conjecture.

### What is proved

Let

\[
E_hx=x+h\,g(x),\qquad B_h=E_h^2,\qquad C_h=E_{2h}.
\]

The paired local defect factors exactly as

\[
B_hx-C_hx
=h\bigl(g(x+hg(x))-g(x)\bigr)
=h^2a_h(x),
\]

where

\[
a_h(x)=\int_0^1Dg(x+shg(x))[g(x)]\,ds.
\]

Using the noncommuting hybrids

\[
H_j=\mathcal O\!\left(C_h^{\,t-j}B_h^j\theta_0\right),
\]

one obtains before taking absolute values

\[
\boxed{\Delta_t(h)=h^2\sum_{j=1}^tA_{j,t}(h)},
\]

with

\[
A_{j,t}(h)=
\int_0^1
D(\mathcal O\circ C_h^{t-j})
\bigl(c_j+\lambda h^2a_h(y_j)\bigr)[a_h(y_j)]\,d\lambda,
\]

\[
y_j=B_h^{j-1}\theta_0,\qquad c_j=C_hy_j.
\]

Writing \(S_t=\sum_jA_{j,t}\), the established OMFP parity makes \(S_t\) odd. Thus, whenever \(A_{j,t}\in C^3\),

\[
\kappa_t=S_t'(0)
=\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{\psi_\alpha,2},
\]

where \(J_{\psi_\alpha,2}\) is the explicit nine-Gaussian-moment activation formula recorded in the proof. Taylor’s integral remainder then gives exactly

\[
\left|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa_th^3\right|
\le
\frac{|h|^5}{6}
\sup_{|u|\le |h|}|S_t'''(u)|.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\sup_{\substack{j\le t\\|u|\le\rho/t}}
|A_{j,t}'''(u)|\le C_A t^3
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\left|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa_th^3\right|
\le\frac{C_A}{6}t^4|h|^5.
\]

This proves the exact paired cancellation and the complete \(t^4\) reduction.

### Why the required norm cannot exist

Suppose the demanded norm

\[
\|V\|_{\mathfrak X}
=\sum_{m\ge0}w_m\|\mathcal D^mV\|_{p_m}
\]

contains constants and the initialized activation atom

\[
X_\alpha=\psi_\alpha(G).
\]

Quantitative same-space product closure would require some finite \(C_\times\) with

\[
\|UV\|_{\mathfrak X}
\le C_\times\|U\|_{\mathfrak X}\|V\|_{\mathfrak X}.
\]

Because constants have no positive source derivatives, definiteness forces \(w_0>0\). If \(p_0=\infty\), the unbounded variable \(X_\alpha\) is excluded. Hence \(p_0<\infty\).

For \(|\alpha|M\le \frac12\),

\[
\frac12\le
\|G+\alpha\varphi(G)\|_2
\le\frac32
\]

and

\[
\|X_\alpha\|_q
\ge
\frac{\|G\|_q-|\alpha|M}{1+|\alpha|M}
\ge c\sqrt q
\]

for all sufficiently large \(q\), with a numerical \(c>0\).

Iterating product closure gives

\[
\|X_\alpha^n\|_{\mathfrak X}
\le
C_\times^{n-1}\|X_\alpha\|_{\mathfrak X}^{n}.
\]

But its source-order-zero term gives

\[
\|X_\alpha^n\|_{\mathfrak X}
\ge
w_0\|X_\alpha^n\|_{p_0}
=
w_0\|X_\alpha\|_{np_0}^{n}.
\]

Taking \(n\)-th roots therefore yields

\[
w_0^{1/n}c\sqrt{np_0}
\le
C_\times^{1-1/n}\|X_\alpha\|_{\mathfrak X},
\]

which is impossible as \(n\to\infty\).

This contradiction uses only \(m=0\). No choices of higher weights, moment exponents, singular-Gram quotients, or source-history aggregation can repair it.

Independently, unrestricted coefficientwise adjoint and product closure would require

\[
w_{m-1}\le C_Jw_m,
\qquad
m w_m\le C_Pw_1w_{m-1},
\]

and hence \(m\le C_PC_Jw_1\) for every \(m\), another contradiction.

### Corrected analytic structure

The viable object is a radius-and-moment scale,

\[
\|V\|_{\rho,p}
=
\sum_{m\ge0}\frac{\rho^m}{m!}
\|\mathcal D^mV\|_{L^p},
\]

for which

\[
\|UV\|_{\rho,p}
\le
\|U\|_{\rho,2p}\|V\|_{\rho,2p},
\]

while the exact adjoint/source shift satisfies only the cross-radius estimate

\[
\|S^kV\|_{\rho',p}
\le
\frac{k!\rho}{(\rho-\rho')^{k+1}}\|V\|_{\rho,p},
\qquad \rho'<\rho.
\]

Chronological simplex factors can cancel \(k!\), but the complete marked \(L=2\) OMFP graph still lacks a proved uniform control of the interspersed products, moving pointers, and coupled \(\bar\rho,\bar\sigma\) responses. A proposed causal charging proof was independently rejected by an explicit same-time nested-response branch.

Therefore the audited status is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{c}
\text{exact paired factorization and conditional }t^4\text{ reduction: proved},\\
\text{requested single-space all-source closure: impossible},\\
\text{uniform scalar }Ct^4|\eta|^5\text{ theorem: still open}.
\end{array}}
\]

The complete proof and audits are in:

- [Independent final audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_uniform_near_identity_l2/FINAL_RESOLUTION_AUDIT.md)

The uniform result fails for every fixed nonzero quadratic component, however small. The obstruction is stronger than \(t^5\): the effective fifth-order remainder coefficient grows faster than every polynomial in \(t\).

Let

\[
\psi(x)=px+qx^2,\qquad
p=\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{\alpha^2+3\beta^2}},\qquad
q=\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{\alpha^2+3\beta^2}},
\]

so that \(\mathbb E\psi(G)^2=1\). Thus “small nonlinear part” means small \(|q|\), equivalently small \(|\beta/\alpha|\), not small linear coefficient \(\alpha\).

Define

\[
\Delta_t(h)=F_{2t}(h)-F_t(2h).
\]

Then the following holds.

### Uniform no-go theorem

For every fixed \(q\neq0\) and every \(\rho>0\),

\[
\boxed{\Delta_t(\rho/t)\longrightarrow+\infty.}
\]

Consequently, if

\[
\kappa_t=[h^3]\Delta_t(h),
\qquad
B_t(\rho)=
\sup_{0<h\le\rho/t}
\frac{|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa_th^3|}{h^5},
\]

then, for every fixed \(r\),

\[
\boxed{\frac{B_t(\rho)}{t^r}\longrightarrow+\infty.}
\]

In particular, neither

\[
|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa_th^3|
\le Ct^4h^5
\]

nor the same inequality with \(t^4\) replaced by any fixed polynomial in \(t\) can hold uniformly on \(0<h\le\rho/t\).

### Why it happens

At finite width, freeze only the bottom-feature update. The remaining top subsystem closes exactly as

\[
A_{k+1}=A_k+h(pZ_k+qZ_k^2),
\]

\[
Z_{k+1}=Z_k+hA_k(p+2qZ_k),
\]

with independent \(A_0,Z_0\sim N(0,1)\) after the width limit.

For \(p,q\ge0\), the full network update, its observable, and this frozen subsystem are polynomials with nonnegative coefficients in the raw initialization variables. Writing the full field as \(g_0+\lambda g_1\), where \(g_1\) is the deleted bottom update, gives

\[
E_h^2-E_{2h}
=
h\bigl(g(x+hg(x))-g(x)\bigr),
\]

whose coefficients are nonnegative. Iteration shows that the entire paired defect is a polynomial in \(\lambda\) with nonnegative coefficients. Gaussian expectation preserves this order because independent Gaussian monomials have either zero or positive expectation. Hence

\[
\Delta_t(h)\ge D_t(h),
\]

where \(D_t\) is the paired defect of the scalar recursion above.

Now select only the quadratic branches \(qZ^2\) and \(2qAZ\). After \(N\) steps, the selected state term has \(h\)-degree \(2^N-1\). At the fine horizon \(N=2t\), the selected output term has

\[
M_t=3(4^t-1)
\]

powers of \(h\), and its raw Gaussian exponents are exactly

\[
(4^t,\,2\cdot4^t).
\]

The coarse \(t\)-step polynomial has maximal degree only \(3(2^t-1)\), so it cannot cancel this term. Therefore

\[
D_t(h)\ge
q^{M_t+1}h^{M_t}
\,\mathbb EG^{4^t}\,
\mathbb EG^{2\cdot4^t}.
\]

Since

\[
\mathbb EG^{2\cdot4^t}
=(2\cdot4^t-1)!!
\ge
\left(\frac{4^t}{\mathrm e}\right)^{4^t},
\]

at \(h=\rho/t\),

\[
D_t(\rho/t)\ge L_t,
\]

where

\[
L_t=
q^{3(4^t-1)+1}
\left(\frac{\rho}{t}\right)^{3(4^t-1)}
\left(\frac{4^t}{\mathrm e}\right)^{4^t}.
\]

Its logarithm satisfies

\[
\frac{\log L_t}{4^t}
=
t\log4-3\log t+O_{q,\rho}(1)
\longrightarrow+\infty.
\]

Thus \(L_t\), and hence \(\Delta_t(\rho/t)\), grows faster than every polynomial.

The exact cubic coefficient remains harmless:

\[
\kappa_t=\frac{t(2t-1)}2J_{p,q},
\]

with

\[
\begin{aligned}
J_{p,q}={}&48p^{12}+3446p^{10}q^2+54820p^8q^4
+387560p^6q^6\\
&+1380876p^4q^8+2399274p^2q^{10}
+1592952q^{12}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Hence

\[
|\kappa_t|(\rho/t)^3\le J_{p,q}\rho^3/t,
\]

which cannot offset \(L_t\).

The signs of \(p,q\) do not matter: two exact orthogonal conjugacies reduce the network to \((|p|,|q|)\).

The width-first qualification is also closed: at each fixed \((N,h)\), expanding

\[
W^s=W^0+\frac h{\sqrt n}\sum_{r<s}C^r(H^r)^T
\]

turns the trained network exactly into a finite reused-\(W^0/(W^0)^\top\), polynomially smooth Gaussian program. Its terminal output converges in every finite \(L^r\). Thus the order is genuinely

\[
n\to\infty\text{ at fixed }(t,h),
\qquad\text{then }t\to\infty,\ h=\rho/t.
\]

This does not contradict the previously computed local result

\[
[h^5]\Delta_t(h)=O(t^4).
\]

The obstruction occurs at order \(3(4^t-1)\), which itself grows with \(t\); no fixed jet can detect it. The identity case \(q=0\) is therefore singular: arbitrarily small but fixed quadratic curvature eventually destroys the uniform positive-time bound.

The fully general claim for the actual \(L=2\) network is not yet proved. The audit produced a complete arbitrary-sign theorem for the frozen mean-field recursion and a full-network theorem for sign-coherent polynomials, but it also found exact counterexamples to both bridges needed for arbitrary signed coefficients.

Assume \(d\ge2\); otherwise the normalized identity activation is an immediate exception.

### 1. Arbitrary signed polynomial: complete frozen-block obstruction

Let

\[
P(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{d}c_jx^j,\qquad c_d>0,\qquad \mathbb E P(G)^2=1,
\]

and consider

\[
A^{+}=A+hP(Z),\qquad Z^{+}=Z+hAP'(Z).
\]

Write

\[
D_t(h)=\mathbb E[A_{2t}P(Z_{2t})]
-\mathbb E[A_t(2h)P(Z_t(2h))].
\]

Set

\[
s=d^{2t},\qquad r=d^{2t-1},\qquad
M=\frac{d+1}{d-1}(s-1).
\]

For even \(d\), define

\[
k_t=M,
\]

\[
b_t=C_{2t}\,\mathbb EG^s\,
\mathbb E\!\left[P(G)^rP'(G)^s\right]>0,
\]

where

\[
C_N=
c_d^{\frac{(d+1)d^{N-1}-2}{d-1}}
d^{\frac{d(d^{N-1}-1)}{d-1}}.
\]

For odd \(d\), let \(a=c_{d-1}/c_d\), set \(k_t=M-1\), and define

\[
b_t
=
sC_{2t}\mathbb EG^{s-1}
\mathbb E\!\left[
P^{r-1}(P')^{s-1}
\left\{
rP'+\left(G+\frac ad\right)P
\right\}
\right].
\]

A tail-versus-compact estimate proves \(b_t>0\) for every sufficiently large \(t\). The odd-degree calculation includes every deficit-one source; equivalently, one first translates by

\[
Y=Z+\frac{c_{d-1}}{dc_d}
\]

to remove the degree-\((d-1)\) coefficient exactly.

For any cubic coefficient \(\kappa_t\), Chebyshev extremality gives the strict bound

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{0\le h\le\rho/t}
|D_t(h)-\kappa_t h^3|
\ge
2^{1-2k_t}b_t\left(\frac{\rho}{t}\right)^{k_t}.
}
\]

Moreover, for an explicit activation-dependent \(C_{P,\rho}\),

\[
\log\!\left[
2^{1-2k_t}b_t\left(\frac{\rho}{t}\right)^{k_t}
\right]
\ge
(d+1)d^{2t}t\log d
-\frac{d+1}{d-1}d^{2t}\log t
-C_{P,\rho}d^{2t}.
\]

Hence

\[
\sup_{0<h\le\rho/t}
\frac{|D_t(h)-\kappa_t h^3|}{t^4h^5}
\longrightarrow\infty.
\]

This proves the desired uniform no-go for every signed polynomial in the frozen mean-field block.

### 2. Full network: proved sign-coherent class

If one of

\[
\varepsilon P(\sigma x),
\qquad \varepsilon,\sigma\in\{\pm1\},
\]

has nonnegative coefficients, coefficientwise bottom-update deletion is valid. Consequently, with \(\Delta_t(h)=F_{2t}(h)-F_t(2h)\),

\[
\Delta_t(H)-\kappa_tH^3
\ge
\begin{cases}
c_d^{M+1}H^M\,\mathbb EG^s\,\mathbb EG^{ds},
& d\ \text{even},\\[1mm]
c_d^MH^{M-1}\,\mathbb EG^{s+1}\,
\mathbb EG^{d(s-1)},
& d\ \text{odd},
\end{cases}
\]

at \(H=\rho/t\). Both lower bounds diverge. This covers nonnegative polynomials, monomials, shifted powers, and their exact sign/reflection conjugates.

### 3. Why arbitrary signed coefficients remain open for the full network

Two exact width-first calculations invalidate the required shortcuts.

For

\[
P_K(x)=b_K(x^2-K),
\qquad
b_K=(K^2-2K+3)^{-1/2},
\]

the actual one-step OMFP output satisfies

\[
\boxed{
[h^7]F_1(h)=1024\,b_K^{16}(3-K)^3.
}
\]

Thus \(P_3(x)=(x^2-3)/\sqrt6\) is normalized, nonlinear, and has positive leading coefficient, yet its maximal full-network \(h\)-sector vanishes.

More decisively, coefficientwise deletion itself is false. For

\[
P(x)=-\frac{11}{9}+\frac{10}{27}x^2,
\qquad \mathbb EP(G)^2=1,
\]

the exact width-first Wick DAG gives

\[
[h^{11}]
\left(\Delta_1^{\mathrm{full}}-\Delta_1^{\mathrm{frozen}}\right)<0.
\]

Therefore the arbitrary-sign scalar lower bound cannot currently be transferred to the full network. These examples refute the intermediate lemmas, not the eventual full-network no-go conjecture. Proving that conjecture requires a new complete signed-OMFP coefficient theorem.

The complete proof and adversarial audit are in:

- [Final theorem scope](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_polynomial_uniform_no_go/FINAL_POLYNOMIAL_SCOPE.md)
- [Signed scalar proof](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_polynomial_uniform_no_go/SIGNED_SCALAR_CHEBYSHEV_THEOREM.md)
- [Full signed-bridge audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_polynomial_uniform_no_go/FULL_SIGNED_BRIDGE_AUDIT.md)

So the rigorous answer is: the strict uniform lower bound is proved for every signed polynomial in the frozen block and for a broad sign-coherent class in the full network; for the actual full network with completely arbitrary signed coefficients, it remains open.

Yes. The universal claim is false for the uniform, nonzero-learning-rate remainder—even for a \(C^\infty\), globally Lipschitz, linearly growing activation.

Define

\[
\Delta_t(h)=F_{2t}(h)-F_t(2h)
\]

and the best possible uniform remainder constant

\[
\mathcal B_\phi(t,\rho)
=
\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb R}
\sup_{0<|h|\le \rho/t}
\frac{|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa h^3|}{|h|^5}.
\]

For every \(\rho>0\), we construct an RMS-normalized activation satisfying

\[
\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb R),
\qquad
0<c_\phi\le \phi'(x)\le C_\phi,
\qquad
|\phi(x)|\le C_\phi(1+|x|),
\]

whose fixed-step width-first outputs all exist and are finite, but for \(t_N=N\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal B_\phi(t_N,\rho)
\ge 10\,t_N^{N+5}.
}
\]

Hence

\[
\frac{\mathcal B_\phi(t_N,\rho)}{t_N^q}\longrightarrow\infty
\]

for every fixed \(q\), particularly \(q=5\).

The decisive calculation is the exact general-\(t\) narrow-bump symbol. A compact \(C^1\)-small perturbation localized near \(X\), with width

\[
w=\delta\sqrt{\gamma(X)},
\]

produces the single-node principal coefficient

\[
-\frac{t(8t^3+3)}{24}\,
r^2sv^4\,
\gamma(X)\frac{\delta^2}{w^3}
\int (p'')^2.
\]

The temporal factors for \(t=1,2,3\) are

\[
-\frac{11}{24},
\qquad
-\frac{67}{12},
\qquad
-\frac{219}{8}.
\]

For the lower hidden layer, the strict response-aware contribution is

\[
-\left(5t^4+\frac{15}{8}t\right)
d^3b^4
\gamma(X)\frac{\delta^2}{w^3}
\int(p'')^2,
\]

where \(d=\mathbb E[\phi_{\rm old}'(G)^2]>0\). Because

\[
\gamma(X)\frac{\delta^2}{w^3}
=
\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{\gamma(X)}},
\]

this tends to infinity while the perturbation’s \(C^1\)-size tends to zero.

The final activation is a locally finite sum

\[
\Phi(x)
=
x+\sum_{N\ge2}
\delta_Nw_N
P\!\left(\frac{x-X_N}{w_N}\right),
\qquad
\phi=\frac{\Phi}{\|\Phi(G)\|_2},
\]

where \(P'=p\), \(p\in C_c^\infty\), and \(\int p=0\). The amplitudes are summable, so \(\phi'\) remains bounded above and away from zero.

At stage \(N\), the smooth truncation is chosen with fifth coefficient \(|\beta_N|\ge20t_N^{N+5}\). Two rates, \(h_N\) and \(h_N/2\), are then preserved under all future \(C^1\)-small insertions. For any proposed cubic coefficient \(\kappa\), writing \(x=(\kappa_N-\kappa)/h_N^2\), the two normalized defects are

\[
x+\beta_N+e_1,
\qquad
4x+\beta_N+e_2.
\]

Since

\[
3|\beta_N|
\le
5\max\{|x+\beta_N|,\ |4x+\beta_N|\},
\]

no choice of \(\kappa\) can cancel both rates. This gives the displayed super-polynomial lower bound using exact fixed-nonzero-step outputs; no cubic or fifth jet of the final activation is assumed.

The all-six-source audit also closes the previously missing \(E_6=\|H^2g\|^2\) sector: the complete general-\(t\) map has 1045 terms and no negative-margin old/new subset. \(E_6\)’s apparent zero-margin terms integrate to \(16-16+0=0\).

One important distinction:

- If “coefficient” means the literal Taylor coefficient \([h^5]\Delta_t\), then whenever it exists it is actually \(O_\phi(t^4)\), because
  \[
  [h^5]\Delta_t
  =
  \sum_{j=1}^5
  \left[
  \binom{2t}{j}-32\binom tj
  \right]\Theta_{5,j},
  \]
  whose degree-five term cancels.
- The counterexample concerns the uniform remainder coefficient on \(0<|h|\le\rho/t\), which is the quantity needed for the gradient-flow limit.
