=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 19401 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.198Z | assistant | final_answer ===
The rigorous answer splits in two.

### Exact ReLU and leaky-ReLU

Write the RMS-normalized activation as

\[
\phi_{a,b}(x)=
\begin{cases}
ax,&x\ge 0,\\
bx,&x<0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
0\le b<a,
\qquad
\frac{a^2+b^2}{2}=1.
\]

For leak \(\lambda\in[0,1)\),

\[
a=\sqrt{\frac{2}{1+\lambda^2}},
\qquad
b=\lambda a.
\]

The audited hard-kink calculation produces

\[
p=Y+2bA,
\qquad
q=Y+2aA,
\]

where

\[
A\sim N(0,1),
\qquad
Y\sim N\!\left(0,e\right),
\qquad
e=\frac{a^4+b^4}{2},
\]

independently. For one switching surface, the exact all-\(t\) paired Euler identity is

\[
J_t(p,q,c)
=
t\,c\,pq\,
\mathbf 1_{\{p>0\ \mathrm{or}\ q<0\}}.
\]

Consequently, the candidate top-gate coefficient is

\[
D_{a,b}
=
\gamma(0)\,
\mathbb E\!\left[
(a-b)A\,pq\,
\mathbf 1_{\{p>0\ \mathrm{or}\ q<0\}}
\right],
\]

or equivalently,

\[
\boxed{
D_{a,b}
=
\frac{3(a-b)}{4\pi\sqrt e}
\int_{2b}^{2a}
\frac{(y-2b)(2a-y)}
     {(1+y^2/e)^{5/2}}\,dy
>0.
}
\]

The lower-layer kink contribution vanishes by Gaussian oddness. For normalized ReLU,

\[
\boxed{
D_{\mathrm{ReLU}}
=
\frac{\sqrt2}{\pi}
\left(1-\frac1{\sqrt5}\right)
\approx0.2488413097.
}
\]

If the remaining nonsmooth OMFP bridges hold, then

\[
\Delta_t(h)
=
F_{2t}(h)-F_t(2h)
=
tD_{a,b}\,h|h|+o_t(h^2).
\]

This would imply

\[
\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb R}
\sup_{0<|h|\le \rho/t}
\frac{|\Delta_t(h)-\kappa h^3|}{|h|^5}
=+\infty
\]

for every \(t\ge1\). Thus the obstruction would be much stronger than either failure of \(o(t^5)\) or finite \(\Omega(t^t)\) growth: the cubic-subtracted fifth quotient would already be infinite at \(t=1\).

However, the adversarial audit found that this is not yet an unconditional theorem for the actual width-first network. Two substantive bridges remain:

1. Fixed-\(h\) identification of the reused-\(W/W^\top\) finite network with an indicator-valued adaptive Gaussian DAG.
2. A marked-source response-intertwining and singular-\(h\) boundary expansion proving that the complete DAG—not merely its initial normal velocities—reduces to the displayed affine-hinge coefficient.

Therefore the exact-network ReLU/leaky-ReLU classification remains conditional/open. A finite-width sign-cell expansion cannot close it because it would exchange the width and learning-rate limits.

### Smooth ReLU-like counterexample

The smooth version is unconditional.

Define a compact \(C^\infty\) transition

\[
\vartheta(x)=
\begin{cases}
0,&x\le0,\\
e^{-1/x},&x>0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
S(x)=
\frac{\vartheta(x+1)}
{\vartheta(x+1)+\vartheta(1-x)},
\]

and

\[
b_{\lambda,\tau}(x)
=
\lambda x
+
(1-\lambda)
\int_{-\infty}^{x}S(y/\tau)\,dy.
\]

Then

\[
b_{\lambda,\tau}(x)=\lambda x
\quad(x\le-\tau),
\qquad
b_{\lambda,\tau}(x)=x
\quad(x\ge\tau).
\]

Starting from this smooth exact-affine-tail ReLU/leaky-ReLU, add the previously audited remote bump ladder:

\[
\Phi(x)
=
b_{\lambda,\tau}(x)
+
\sum_{m\ge2}
\delta_m w_m
P\!\left(\frac{x-X_m}{w_m}\right),
\qquad
w_m=\delta_m\sqrt{\gamma(X_m)},
\]

and normalize

\[
\widetilde\phi
=
\frac{\Phi}{\|\Phi(G)\|_2}.
\]

The supports are disjoint and tend to \(+\infty\), so the sum is locally finite. Hence \(\widetilde\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb R)\), is globally Lipschitz and linearly growing, and can be made arbitrarily close in weighted \(C^1\) to the smooth ReLU/leaky-ReLU base.

For every \(\lambda\in[0,1)\), \(\tau,\varepsilon,\rho>0\), the amplitudes can be chosen so that

\[
d_1\!\left(
\widetilde\phi,
\frac{b_{\lambda,\tau}}{\|b_{\lambda,\tau}(G)\|_2}
\right)<\varepsilon
\]

and, simultaneously,

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal B_{\widetilde\phi}(N,\rho)
\ge
\frac35N^{N+6}-2
\ge N^N,
\qquad N\ge2.
}
\]

Thus there are genuinely \(C^\infty\), bounded-slope, ReLU-looking activations whose exact width-first uniform remainder violates \(o(t^5)\) by at least \(\Omega(t^t)\). This does not prove that ordinary softplus or an unperturbed single-scale smoothing violates the property.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 19695 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.207Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Assuming the conventional half-squared loss

\[
\ell(f)=\frac12(1-f)^2,
\]

loss descent changes the picture substantially: the fine/coarse discrepancy generically begins at order \(\eta^2\). This is ordinary first-order Euler error and is compatible with a continuous-time limit.

### Exact reduction

Define

\[
P(\theta)=f(\theta)-\frac12f(\theta)^2.
\]

Then

\[
-\nabla\ell=(1-f)\nabla f=\nabla P,
\qquad
\ell=\frac12-P.
\]

Thus one loss-gradient step is exactly Euler ascent for \(P\):

\[
E_\eta(\theta)=\theta+\eta\nabla P(\theta).
\]

Let

\[
D_t^\ell(\eta)
=
\ell(E_\eta^{2t}\theta_0)
-
\ell(E_{2\eta}^{t}\theta_0).
\]

This is not constant-step feature ascent: the effective feature step \(\eta(1-f)\) is recomputed at every iteration.

### Complete expansion through order five

For a scalar function \(u\), define

\[
T_k u=\frac1{k!}D^ku[v,\ldots,v],
\qquad
v=\nabla P,
\]

and

\[
\Lambda_{m,r}
=
\sum_{\substack{k_1+\cdots+k_r=m\\k_i\ge1}}
(T_{k_1}\cdots T_{k_r}\ell)(\theta_0).
\]

Then, whenever the width-first population DAG is \(C^6\) on the required segment,

\[
\boxed{
D_t^\ell(\eta)
=
\sum_{m=2}^{5}\eta^m
\sum_{r=1}^{m}
\left[
{2t\choose r}
-
2^m{t\choose r}
\right]\Lambda_{m,r}
+
R_{6,t}(\eta),
}
\]

with the exact integral remainder

\[
R_{6,t}(\eta)
=
\frac{\eta^6}{5!}
\int_0^1(1-s)^5
\frac{d^6}{d\eta^6}D_t^\ell(s\eta)\,ds.
\]

Every \(\Lambda_{m,r}\) is an initialization quantity and can be reduced by Gaussian peeling; it is not defined from the trained trajectory.

For the \(q=1,L=2\) network, let

\[
d=\mathbb E[\phi'(G)^2],
\qquad
\Theta=1+d+d^2,
\]

and let the already established cubic invariants be

\[
\mathsf S_{\phi,2}
=
D^3f[g,g,g],
\qquad
\mathsf H_{\phi,2}
=
\|Dg[g]\|^2,
\qquad g=\nabla f.
\]

Readout symmetry gives \(D^2f[g,g]=0\) at initialization. Hence

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
D_t^\ell(\eta)
={}&t\Theta^2\eta^2\\
&-\frac{t(2t-1)}2
\left(
4\Theta^3+\mathsf S_{\phi,2}+4\mathsf H_{\phi,2}
\right)\eta^3\\
&+K_{4,\phi}(t)\eta^4
+K_{5,\phi}(t)\eta^5
+R_{6,t}(\eta),
\end{aligned}
}
\]

where

\[
\deg K_{4,\phi}\le3,
\qquad
\deg K_{5,\phi}\le4.
\]

The explicit Gaussian-integral formulas for \(\mathsf S_{\phi,2}\), \(\mathsf H_{\phi,2}\), \(K_{4,\phi}\), and \(K_{5,\phi}\) are recorded in the study note linked below.

### ReLU and leaky-ReLU

For normalized ReLU/leaky-ReLU,

\[
d=1,\qquad \Theta=3.
\]

The smooth formula is not directly valid at the kink. Conditional on the same two open hard-kink OMFP bridges identified previously, the Gaussian boundary term changes sign because \(\ell'(0)=-1\). This gives

\[
\boxed{
D_t^\ell(\eta)
=
9t\eta^2
-
tD_{a,b}\eta|\eta|
+
o_t(\eta^2),
}
\]

where

\[
D_{a,b}
=
\frac{3(a-b)}{4\pi\sqrt e}
\int_{2b}^{2a}
\frac{(y-2b)(2a-y)}
     {(1+y^2/e)^{5/2}}\,dy,
\qquad
e=\frac{a^4+b^4}{2}.
\]

For positive \(\eta\),

\[
D_t^\ell(\eta)
=
t(9-D_{a,b})\eta^2+o_t(\eta^2).
\]

For ReLU,

\[
D_{\mathrm{ReLU}}
=
\frac{\sqrt2}{\pi}
\left(1-\frac1{\sqrt5}\right),
\]

so the candidate coefficient is

\[
9-D_{\mathrm{ReLU}}
\approx 8.75115869.
\]

This hard-ReLU formula remains conditional for the actual width-first network.

### Does this violate the continuous-time limit?

No. At fixed physical time

\[
T=2t\eta,
\]

the leading loss discrepancy is

\[
t\eta^2=\frac{T^2}{4t}=O(t^{-1}).
\]

More generally, Euler chronology implies that the order-\(\eta^m\) paired coefficient has degree at most \(m-1\) in \(t\). Therefore every fixed Taylor order satisfies

\[
t^{m-1}\eta^m=O(t^{-1})
\qquad
\left(\eta=\frac{T}{2t}\right).
\]

So neither the ReLU kink nor the previously constructed super-large fifth coefficient is a continuous-time obstruction.

But no finite expansion—even the complete fifth-order expansion—proves convergence, because \(R_{6,t}\) may be nonuniform in \(t\).

A sufficient noncircular state estimate would be: on an activation-envelope-certified reachable set,

\[
\|v\|\le M_T,\qquad
\operatorname{Lip}(v)\le L_T,\qquad
\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\le R_T.
\]

Then an exact hybrid telescoping argument gives

\[
\boxed{
|D_t^\ell(\eta)|
\le
R_TL_TM_T\,
t\eta^2e^{2L_Tt|\eta|}.
}
\]

At \(\eta=T/(2t)\),

\[
|D_t^\ell(T/(2t))|
\le
\frac{R_TL_TM_TT^2e^{L_TT}}{4t},
\]

which is summable along dyadic refinements and proves scalar mesh convergence.

Those reachable-state bounds have not yet been proved for nonlinear reused-matrix OMFP, and classical Lipschitz control does not apply directly to hard ReLU gates. Therefore the definitive conclusion is:

- the loss discrepancy is consistent with a normal first-order continuous-time limit;
- it contains no proved obstruction;
- the actual nonlinear width-first ReLU/leaky-ReLU loss-gradient limit remains open, because an all-source reachable stability theorem is still missing.

If you use the unhalved loss \((1-f)^2\), replace \(\eta\) by \(2\eta\) and multiply the displayed loss discrepancy by \(2\).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 20181 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.222Z | assistant | final_answer ===
The two activations have different verdicts.

| Activation | Full compact-time mesh guarantee | Continuous-time conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| \(\phi(x)=x^2/\sqrt3\) | **Fails rigorously** | No continuous width-first gradient-flow trace from initialization |
| \(\phi(x)=\sqrt2\,x_+\) | **Open for Euler/Filippov meshes** | A fixed-\(\phi'(0)\) classical flow is not well posed |

Here “full mesh guarantee” means uniform compact-time convergence of the interpolated width-first Euler losses—not merely agreement at one terminal time.

### Quadratic activation: rigorous failure

Let \(F_k^h\) be the width-first output after \(k\) half-MSE steps of size \(h\), and let \(\mathcal F_m(s_0,\ldots,s_{m-1})\) denote the width-first feature-ascent output with deterministic nonnegative step schedule \(s\).

For pure \(x^2\), every finite-width feature trajectory and terminal output is a polynomial with nonnegative formal coefficients in the initialization variables and in the \(s_j\). Gaussian expectation eliminates odd monomials and is positive on every even monomial. Hence

\[
s_j\le \widetilde s_j\quad\forall j
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal F_m(s)\le \mathcal F_m(\widetilde s).
\]

The fixed-schedule polynomial OMFP theorem lets this inequality pass through \(n\to\infty\).

Loss descent is exactly an adaptive feature schedule:

\[
F_k^h=\mathcal F_k(s_0,\ldots,s_{k-1}),
\qquad
s_k=h(1-F_k^h).
\]

Fix \(0<\delta<1\). Until \(F_k^h\) first reaches \(\delta\),

\[
s_k\ge (1-\delta)h>0.
\]

But the previously proved all-orders quadratic result gives, for every \(\rho>0\),

\[
\mathcal F_{2t}\left(\frac{\rho}{t},\ldots,\frac{\rho}{t}\right)
\longrightarrow+\infty .
\]

Now set \(h_N=T/N\). Given any \(\varepsilon>0\), choose

\[
t_N=\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_N}\right\rfloor,
\qquad
\rho_*=\frac{(1-\delta)\varepsilon}{4}.
\]

If no hit occurred before \(2t_N\), schedule monotonicity would imply

\[
F_{2t_N}^{h_N}
\ge
\mathcal F_{2t_N}
\left(\frac{\rho_*}{t_N},\ldots,\frac{\rho_*}{t_N}\right)
\longrightarrow+\infty,
\]

contradicting \(F_{2t_N}^{h_N}<\delta\). Therefore the first hitting time satisfies

\[
\tau_N(\delta)
=
h_N\min\{k:F_k^{h_N}\ge\delta\}
\longrightarrow0.
\]

Thus every continuous predictor interpolation reaches the fixed value \(\delta\) at times tending to zero, although \(F_0=0\). Its loss simultaneously reaches

\[
\frac12(1-\delta)^2\ne\frac12=\ell(0).
\]

Consequently the meshes cannot converge uniformly on any compact interval to a continuous trajectory with the correct initial condition. In particular, no dyadically summable split-mesh estimate controlling the whole loss path can hold.

This keeps the required order \(n\to\infty\) first and uses an all-orders comparison, not a finite jet.

A crucial qualification is that this does **not** prove

\[
\ell_{2t}(\eta)-\ell_t(2\eta)\not\to0
\]

at one fixed positive terminal time. Both meshes could traverse the same instantaneous initial layer and subsequently agree. Hence terminal agreement alone would not establish a continuous gradient-flow limit.

### ReLU: classical failure, generalized mesh limit open

For normalized ReLU, take \(c=\sqrt2\). At width \(n=2\), consider

\[
u=(1,1),\quad
W_1=(1,-1),\quad
W_2=(2,0),
\]

\[
a_1=-\frac1c,\qquad
a_2=\frac1{4c}.
\]

Then

\[
z_1=0,\qquad z_2=2,\qquad f=\frac14,\qquad r=1-f=\frac34.
\]

If \(s\) denotes the selected top-gate slope, direct differentiation of both the connector and lower-feature updates gives

\[
\dot z_1=r(4a_1s+2a_2c).
\]

The two one-sided velocities are therefore

\[
p=\frac38>0,
\qquad
q=-\frac{21}{8}<0.
\]

Both sides point toward the gate. With the standard convention \(\phi'(0)=0\), the assigned gate velocity equals \(p\ne0\). A local \(|z|\)-argument proves that no absolutely continuous classical continuation exists: off the gate, \(|z|\) must strictly decrease, forcing \(z\equiv0\), while the nonzero assigned gate velocity forbids \(z\equiv0\).

A transversal flow-tube argument turns this contact into an open set of initial parameters whose trajectories hit the attracting gate. Gaussian initialization assigns that open set positive probability. The same construction works for any fixed value of \(\phi'(0)\) after varying \(a_1\) away from the unique exceptional value.

So a fixed derivative convention does not define a globally well-posed classical ReLU loss flow.

However, this does not disprove an Euler-selected generalized limit. For frozen \(p>0>q\), the Euler gate chatters in an \(O(h)\) strip and has the exact positive-side occupation

\[
\lambda_+=\frac{p}{p-q},
\]

with

\[
\left|
\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbf 1_{\{z_k>0\}}
-N\lambda_+
\right|<1.
\]

Thus the scalar gate has a canonical Filippov-type weak limit. But the network needs more than its mean occupation: since \(I_k^2=I_k\),

\[
I_h\rightharpoonup\lambda_+,
\qquad
I_h^2\rightharpoonup\lambda_+,
\]

not \(\lambda_+^2\). The loss rate sees these second moments through

\[
\|A\phi'(Z)\|_2^2
\quad\text{and}\quad
\|G^*(A\phi'(Z))\|_2^2.
\]

Therefore a valid ReLU theorem requires an occupation/Young-measure OMFP retaining reused \(G/G^*\) responses.

Positive homogeneity does give conditional compact-time norm bounds, so ordinary parameter explosion is not the obstacle. The unresolved bridges are:

1. fixed-mesh width identification for the adaptive hard-indicator program;
2. an occupation-augmented reused-adjoint OMFP;
3. mesh-uniform tail and sewing estimates;
4. uniqueness and restartability of the resulting loss trace.

Accordingly, exact ReLU currently neither passes nor is disproved for the generalized width-first Euler mesh. The classical fixed-derivative flow is rigorously ill posed; the Euler/Filippov limit remains genuinely open.

The combined proof and adversarially audited claim boundary are in [FINAL_X2_RELU_MESH_VERDICT.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_loss_mesh_resolution/FINAL_X2_RELU_MESH_VERDICT.md). Supporting details are in the [quadratic initial-layer theorem](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_quadratic_loss_initial_layer/INITIAL_LAYER_THEOREM.md) and [ReLU compact-time audit](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_loss_time_doubling/RELU_COMPACT_TIME_AUDIT.md).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 20818 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.242Z | assistant | final_answer ===
## Verdict

For the network

\[
f_n(\theta)
=
\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n
a_i\,
\phi\!\left(
\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{j=1}^nW_{ij}\phi(u_j)
\right),
\qquad
\ell_n=\frac12(1-f_n)^2,
\]

with loss-gradient Euler updates

\[
\theta_{k+1}
=
\theta_k+\eta(1-f_n(\theta_k))\,n\nabla f_n(\theta_k),
\qquad t=k\eta,
\]

the conclusions are:

| Activation | Width-first continuous-time limit | Joint \((n,\eta)\) limit |
|---|---|---|
| \(\phi(x)=x^2/\sqrt3\) | **No.** A fixed output/loss change occurs at times tending to zero. | **No for every ODE-resolving scaling.** Arbitrarily under-resolved numerical diagonals remain unclassified. |
| \(\phi(x)=\sqrt2\,x_+\) | Classical ODE: **no**, because it is not globally well posed. Euler-selected generalized flow: **open**. | Scalar predictor/loss are locally precompact for every \(\eta(n)\to0\), but uniqueness, identification, and global continuation are open. |

### 1. Quadratic: the full mesh guarantee fails

Let \(F_k^h\) denote the width-first output. Loss descent is feature ascent with adaptive step

\[
s_k=h(1-F_k^h).
\]

Before \(F_k^h\) reaches any fixed \(\delta\in(0,1)\),

\[
s_k\ge (1-\delta)h.
\]

For deterministic nonnegative schedules, the quadratic expected output is coordinatewise monotone in every step size. The audited all-order result gives

\[
\mathcal F_{2t}\left(\frac{\rho}{t},\ldots,\frac{\rho}{t}\right)
\longrightarrow+\infty
\qquad(\rho>0).
\]

Taking \(h_N=T/N\) and \(t_N=\lfloor\varepsilon/(2h_N)\rfloor\), monotonicity implies that the loss trajectory must cross \(\delta\) before time \(\varepsilon\), for all sufficiently large \(N\). Hence its first crossing time satisfies

\[
\tau_N(\delta)\longrightarrow0.
\]

At that time,

\[
F_N(\tau_N)=\delta,
\qquad
\ell_N(\tau_N)=\frac12(1-\delta)^2,
\]

whereas initialization gives \(F_N(0)=0\) and \(\ell_N(0)=1/2\). Therefore neither predictor nor loss can converge in \(C([0,T])\) with the initialized trace.

This also shows why the terminal scalar comparison

\[
\ell_{2t}(\eta)-\ell_t(2\eta)
\]

is insufficient: both meshes could traverse the same initial layer and later agree. The terminal comparison remains unresolved, but the full path guarantee is definitively false.

### 2. Quadratic: shrinking \(\eta\) jointly with width does not cure it

Let \(f_n^{\mathrm{ode}}\) be the actual finite-width loss flow. There are \(\delta_0>0\) and random times

\[
\tau_n\le \frac{2}{\sqrt{\log n}}
\]

such that, with probability tending to one,

\[
f_n^{\mathrm{ode}}(\tau_n)-f_n^{\mathrm{ode}}(0)\ge\delta_0.
\]

Consequently, every joint scaling satisfying the genuine Euler-consistency condition

\[
\sup_{0\le t\le2/\sqrt{\log n}}
\left|
\bar f_n^{\eta_n}(t)-f_n^{\mathrm{ode}}(t)
\right|
\xrightarrow{\mathbb P}0
\]

inherits the same vanishing-time jump and cannot converge to a continuous initialized limit.

An explicit sufficient condition for this Euler consistency is

\[
\boxed{
\eta_n n^{14}
\exp\!\left(
\frac{6\cdot10^{11}n^9}{\sqrt{\log n}}
\right)
\longrightarrow0.
}
\]

For example, \(\eta_n=e^{-10^{12}n^9}\) qualifies—and still fails to produce a continuous initialized limit.

Thus, under the standard clock \(t=k\eta_n\), making \(\eta_n\) smaller only resolves the finite-width flow more accurately; it cannot remove the underlying width-dependent initial layer.

A deliberately under-resolved intermediate diagonal is not completely ruled out. But any limit obtained there would require an independent consistency theorem and would be a discretization-selected regularization, not presently a joint limit of the canonical finite-width gradient flow.

### 3. ReLU: classical flow fails, but continuous Euler subsequences exist

At width two there is an explicit attracting gate with one-sided normal velocities

\[
p=\frac38>0,
\qquad
q=-\frac{21}{8}<0.
\]

Both sides point toward the gate. Any fixed choice of \(\phi'(0)\) assigns, generically, a nonzero velocity on the gate, producing no absolutely continuous classical continuation. An open set of Gaussian initializations reaches this obstruction. Hence the fixed-convention classical ReLU gradient ODE is not globally well posed.

However, this does not imply a discontinuous Euler limit. In fact, for every deterministic \(\eta_n\to0\), define

\[
R=\max\left\{
1,\|a\|_n,\|u\|_n,
\left\|\frac W{\sqrt n}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\right\}.
\]

The exact ReLU Euler update obeys

\[
R^+\le R+6\eta R^5.
\]

On a high-probability initialization event, this gives \(R\le12\) on the explicit interval

\[
0\le t\le T_0,
\qquad
T_0=\frac1{384\cdot6^4}.
\]

Moreover,

\[
|f^+-f|\le 60\cdot12^7\eta.
\]

Therefore the predictor and loss interpolants are uniformly bounded and equi-Lipschitz on \([0,T_0]\). Their laws are tight in \(C([0,T_0])\), and since

\[
\mathbb E f_n(0)^2=\frac1n,
\]

every subsequential limit is continuous and begins at

\[
F(0)=0,
\qquad
L(0)=\frac12.
\]

So ReLU does not have the quadratic scalar initial-jump obstruction. What remains unproved is whether all subsequences give the same deterministic path and whether the required occupation-augmented OMFP state is unique and restartable.

There is currently no proved \(\eta(n)\) guaranteeing that canonical generalized limit. The initialization slab scale \(n\eta_n\) can be calculated exactly, but it is not sufficient: attracting gates create temporal Young measures whose joint correlations are visible to reused adjoints and squared tangent-kernel terms.

### 4. Ways to obtain an actual continuous limit

Three mathematically valid modifications are available:

1. **Use normalized arctangent.**  
   For the same depth-two feature-learning scaling, normalized \(\arctan\) has a proved unique global restartable pointed-action IDE, with compact-time convergence of predictors, tangent kernels, and losses.

2. **Kernel-normalize the quadratic optimizer.**  
   With
   \[
   K_n=Df_n[g_n]=\frac1n\|g_n\|_2^2,
   \qquad
   \dot\theta=\frac{(1-f_n)g_n}{K_n},
   \]
   one obtains exactly
   \[
   \dot f_n=1-f_n,\qquad
   f_n(t)=1-(1-f_n(0))e^{-t},\qquad
   \ell_n(t)=\ell_n(0)e^{-2t}.
   \]
   This fixes the predictor/loss clock, although it is a different optimizer.

3. **Slow the physical vector field separately.**  
   Multiplying the quadratic vector field by \(\alpha_n\) with
   \[
   \alpha_n n^{14}\to0
   \]
   produces a rigorously frozen predictor/loss limit. This is trivial and changes physical time; it is not achieved merely by shrinking the Euler mesh.

The complete audited synthesis is in [FULL_RESOLUTION_AND_JOINT_SCALING.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_loss_mesh_resolution/FULL_RESOLUTION_AND_JOINT_SCALING.md). Detailed proofs are in [JOINT_SCALING_VERDICT.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_joint_width_mesh_quadratic/JOINT_SCALING_VERDICT.md) and [RELU_NO_CONTINUOUS_LIMIT_TEST.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_loss_time_doubling/RELU_NO_CONTINUOUS_LIMIT_TEST.md).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 21026 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.248Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Short answer: the construction is mostly correct, but it describes a different initialization ensemble. The learning-rate powers are essentially the same as ours; the positive result comes from replacing the iid Gaussian connector by a coherent neuron-embedded kernel.

## Exact comparison

For our normalized quadratic activation \(\phi(x)=x^2/\sqrt3\), set

\[
a_i=u_i,\qquad
b_{ji}=\frac{W_{ji}}{\sqrt{3n}},\qquad
c_j=\frac{a_j^{\mathrm{out}}}{n\sqrt3}.
\]

Then our network becomes exactly

\[
f_n=\sum_j c_j\left(\sum_i b_{ji}a_i^2\right)^2.
\]

Ignoring fixed \(\sqrt3\) factors and the factor two between half-loss and full loss, the original muP update induces

\[
(\eta_a,\eta_b,\eta_c)=(nh,h,h/n).
\]

So that mobility ratio is not a competing scaling—it is our muP scaling expressed in the effective coordinates \((a,b,c)\).

What differs is:

| | Original iid OMFP | Proposed nested model |
|---|---:|---:|
| \(\operatorname{Var}(b_{ji})\) | \(\Theta(n^{-1})\) | \(\Theta(n^{-2})\) |
| Connector | iid Gaussian | \(W_0(\beta_j,\alpha_i)/n\) |
| Preactivation | Gaussian/CLT sum | Coherent LLN integral |
| Layer dependence | Independent | Correlated through neuron types |
| Witness \(F_0\) | \(F_0\to0\) | \(F_0=1/2\) |

In the nested model,

\[
Z_j=\frac1n\sum_i
W_0(\beta_j,\alpha_i)A_0(\alpha_i)^2
\longrightarrow
\int W_0(\beta_j,\alpha)A_0(\alpha)^2\,d\mu(\alpha),
\]

which can be nonzero because \(W_0\) and \(A_0^2\) share the same latent type.

By contrast, if \(b_{ji}\) were genuinely independent and centered with variance \(\sigma^2/n^2\), then

\[
\mathbb E[Z_j^2\mid a]
=
\frac{\sigma^2}{n^2}\sum_i a_i^4
=
O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-1}),
\]

so \(Z_j\to0\). The \(n^{-2}\) variance works only because of the nested correlations.

## What checks out

I verified the following parts of the proposed derivation:

- All three raw gradients are correct.
- The simultaneous finite-step map and exact \(z^+-z\) identity are correct.
- The normalized updates for \(A,W,C\) are exact.
- The \(K_n\) and \(\Gamma_n\) expressions are correct.
- The one-step, two-step, and fixed-\(k\) loss coefficients are correct.
- The centered finite-type witness and all its stated variances and velocities are correct.
- The finite-type Euler-to-ODE argument is valid.
- The finite-type global-existence argument is valid.

The witness is “centered” only marginally. In fact,

\[
W_{ji}=v_j\left(x_i^2-\frac52\right)
\]

is rank one, \(W_{ji}\) is correlated with \(A_i^2=x_i^2\), and \(C_j\) is correlated with the same row type \(v_j\). Those dependencies are exactly what produce \(Z_0\neq0\) and \(F_0=1/2\).

## Three corrections

First, \(nh_n\to0\) is unnecessary for the nested finite-type theorem. The normalized increments are already \(O(h_n)\), so

\[
h_n\to0
\]

is sufficient. The stronger condition \(nh_n\to0\) merely forces the nominal raw number \(\eta_{a,n}=nh_n\) itself to vanish.

Second, the precise finite-type theorem is

\[
\max_{kh_n\le T}
\|x_{n,k}-x(kh_n)\|
\le
C_T(h_n+\delta_n),
\]

where \(x\) is the fixed-dimensional type-reduced state and

\[
\delta_n=\|\mu_n-\mu\|_1+\|\nu_n-\nu\|_1.
\]

Thus \(\delta_n=O(n^{-1})\) for deterministically rounded type counts and \(O_{\mathbb P}(n^{-1/2})\) for sampled finite types. This should not be interpreted as an unnormalized Euclidean estimate for the complete \(n+n^2+n\) parameter array.

Third, the proof given establishes the theorem for fixed finite type spaces with positive type masses. Arbitrary measurable latent spaces require an additional quadrature/empirical-process and stability theorem.

The fixed-\(k\) remainder \(O_k(h^3)\) is also uniform in width only while the normalized type states remain uniformly bounded. It cannot automatically be transferred to the iid Gaussian model.

## Comparison with our joint-scaling result

Your proposed requirement

\[
nh_n\to0
\]

is far weaker than our explicit iid-Gaussian ODE-shadowing condition

\[
h_n n^{14}
\exp\!\left(
\frac{6\cdot10^{11}n^9}{\sqrt{\log n}}
\right)
\to0.
\]

There is no contradiction:

- The nested finite-type vector field has width-independent local Lipschitz constants.
- The iid Gaussian model contains extreme rows/columns whose effective rates grow with \(n\).
- Our condition was a sufficient condition under which Euler follows the finite-width Gaussian ODE—and therefore inherits its initial-layer failure. It was not a proposed positive scaling.

Accordingly, \(h_n=n^{-3}\) rigorously gives the claimed limit for the finite-type nested model. It does not establish anything positive about the original iid Gaussian model; that precise iid diagonal is not covered by the present all-diagonal theorem.

## Final assessment

I agree with the result after rewriting its main conclusion as:

> The mobility \(n:1:n^{-1}\), combined with a bounded finite-type neuron-embedded \(1/n\) connector, yields a rigorous joint mean-field/gradient-flow limit. This is a graphon/nested mean-field model, not the original iid-Gaussian OMFP model.

For the iid centered connector, variance \(\Theta(n^{-1})\) is required, recovering our original Gaussian-action regime. The row-projection argument correctly proves that an ordinary entrywise empirical law cannot close that regime. It does not rule out enriched operator/response descriptions—OMFP is precisely such a description—but our quadratic initial-layer theorem does rule out a classical continuous initialized limit in the width-first and proved ODE-consistent regimes.

The detailed independent audit is [INDEPENDENT_COMPARISON.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_scaling_comparison/INDEPENDENT_COMPARISON.md).

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 21207 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.254Z | assistant | final_answer ===
The current arctangent theorem is correct for the theorem it actually states, but it does not prove every possible meaning of “width-first continuous-time limit.”

| Claim | Status |
|---|---|
| Width limit of the exact finite-width gradient-flow ODE | Proved |
| Width-first auxiliary Euler scheme in the natural coordinate, then mesh removal | Proved |
| Width-first actual discrete GD at fixed \(\eta\), followed by \(\eta\to0\) | Open |
| Earlier direct-Hölder proof of kernel uniform integrability | Broken, then repaired |

So if the repository claimed

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\{\text{finite-width continuous gradient flow}\},
\]

the claim survives. If it claimed

\[
\lim_{\eta\to0}
\left[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\{\text{actual discrete GD with step }\eta\}
\right],
\]

that was too strong.

## MFP reconstruction of the valid theorem

Use

\[
X=\arctan(u),\qquad
Z=GX,\qquad
Y=\arctan(Z),\qquad
f_n=\langle A,Y\rangle_n,
\]

where \(G_0=W_0/\sqrt n\) and \(A_0,u_0,W_0\) are mutually independent standard Gaussians. Define

\[
B=\frac{A}{1+Z^2},
\qquad
Q=G^*B.
\]

Under feature ascent, with the normalized vector metrics and Frobenius matrix metric,

\[
A'=Y,\qquad
u'=\frac{Q}{1+u^2},\qquad
G'=B\otimes X,
\]

where

\[
(b\otimes x)v=b\langle x,v\rangle_n
=\frac1n bx^{\mathsf T}v.
\]

### 1. Natural-coordinate peeling

Set

\[
\rho=\Theta(u):=u+\frac{u^3}{3}.
\]

Since

\[
\Theta'(u)=1+u^2=\frac1{\phi'(u)},
\]

the inner derivative disappears exactly:

\[
\rho'=Q.
\]

Writing \(u=\Theta^{-1}(\rho)\), define

\[
\Psi(\rho)=\arctan(\Theta^{-1}(\rho)),
\qquad
c(\rho)=\frac1{1+\Theta^{-1}(\rho)^2}.
\]

Then

\[
\Psi'(\rho)=c(\rho)^2,
\]

and the feature system becomes

\[
A'=Y,\qquad
\rho'=Q,\qquad
G'=B\otimes X.
\]

All coordinate nonlinearities except the Gaussian multiplier \(A\) are now bounded and globally Lipschitz.

### 2. Exact MFP operator DAG

Write

\[
G=G_0+q,
\qquad
q(s)=\int_0^s B(\sigma)\otimes X(\sigma)\,d\sigma.
\]

On an auxiliary Euler mesh \(h\),

\[
q^k=h\sum_{\ell<k}B^\ell\otimes X^\ell.
\]

Therefore the forward and reused-adjoint nodes peel as

\[
Z^k
=
G_0X^k
+
h\sum_{\ell<k}
B^\ell\langle X^\ell,X^k\rangle_n,
\]

\[
Q^k
=
G_0^*B^k
+
h\sum_{\ell<k}
X^\ell\langle B^\ell,B^k\rangle_n.
\]

These are precisely the MFP response formulas. Every reuse of \(G_0\) and \(G_0^*\) remains in one shared two-sided Gaussian source; nothing is refreshed.

For every fixed mesh, this is a finite MFP program. Its width limit produces an immutable source

\[
\mathfrak G=(H_R,H_C;a_0,\rho_0,\Gamma),
\]

where

\[
\Gamma:H_C\to H_R,\qquad \|\Gamma\|\le2,
\]

and the reverse action is its genuine Hilbert adjoint \(\Gamma^*\).

The limiting autonomous OMFP equation is

\[
A'=Y,\qquad
\rho'=Q,\qquad
q'=B\otimes X,\qquad
G=\Gamma+q.
\]

The source \(\Gamma\) is fixed at initialization; \(q\) is the single current trace-class learned operator. Hence the state is restartable and contains no two-time response kernel.

### 3. Output and tangent kernel

Differentiating the MFP DAG gives

\[
X'=c(\rho)^2Q,
\]

\[
Z'=B\,\mathbb E[X^2]+G\{c(\rho)^2Q\}.
\]

Consequently,

\[
F'(s)=K(s),
\]

with

\[
K
=
\mathbb E[Y^2]
+
\mathbb E[B^2]\mathbb E[X^2]
+
\mathbb E[c(\rho)^2Q^2]
\ge0.
\]

This is the infinite-width limit of the exact finite identity.

### 4. Global well-posedness

Since \(|X|,|Y|\le\pi/2\) and \(|B|\le|A|\),

\[
\|A(s)\|_2
\le
\|a_0\|_2+\frac{\pi}{2}|s|,
\]

\[
\|q(s)\|_1
\le
\frac{\pi}{2}
\left(
\|a_0\|_2|s|+\frac{\pi}{4}s^2
\right),
\]

and \(\|\rho(s)\|_2\) is bounded on every compact feature-time interval. Thus there is no finite-time escape.

The only delicate map is

\[
A\,d(Z),\qquad d(z)=\frac1{1+z^2},
\]

because \(A=a_0+\) bounded and \(a_0\) is Gaussian rather than \(L^\infty\). For bounded Lipschitz \(h\),

\[
\|a_0[h(z)-h(\widetilde z)]\|_2
\le
C\delta\sqrt{\log(e/\delta)},
\qquad
\delta=\|z-\widetilde z\|_2.
\]

Because

\[
\int_{0^+}
\frac{d\delta}
{\delta\sqrt{\log(e/\delta)}}=\infty,
\]

Bihari–Osgood gives uniqueness in the reachable Gaussian-envelope class. Existence follows by cutting off \(a_0\), solving the Lipschitz system, and removing the cutoff; the stability loss \(e^{C_SM}\) is dominated by the Gaussian clipping tail \(e^{-M^2/4}\).

### 5. Width identification and the repaired step

At a fixed readout cutoff and fixed auxiliary mesh:

- the MFP DAG above has a deterministic width limit;
- both finite- and infinite-width exact flows differ from their auxiliary Euler schemes by \(O(h)\), uniformly in width.

The raw term

\[
\mathbb E[c(\rho)^2Q^2]
\]

requires square uniform integrability. The earlier proof incorrectly tried to use a \((2+\varepsilon)\)-moment constant uniformly as \(h\to0\).

The repaired argument uses

\[
q^2\mathbf 1_{\{|q|>R\}}
\le
4(q-v)^2
+
2v^2\mathbf 1_{\{|v|>R/2\}}.
\]

One first fixes an auxiliary mesh. MFP then supplies its finite \((2+\varepsilon)\)-moments. The \(O(h)\) Euler comparison transfers these tails to the exact continuous trajectory. Only after square-tail tightness has been established does one introduce a separate comparison mesh, truncate at fixed \(R\), and remove that mesh. No moment constant uniform in the number of steps is assumed.

This closes convergence of \(K_n\), not merely of the bounded predictor.

### 6. Loss-gradient clock

For the full squared loss, define feature time by

\[
\dot s=2\eta e,
\qquad
e=y-F(s).
\]

Then

\[
\dot e=-2\eta eK(s),
\]

so

\[
e(t)
=
y\exp\left(
-2\eta\int_0^tK(s(\tau))\,d\tau
\right),
\]

and

\[
|s(t)|\le2\eta|y|t.
\]

Thus the physical trajectory is global and unique, and

\[
f(t)+e(t)=y,
\qquad
\dot{\mathcal L}(t)=-4\eta K(t)\mathcal L(t).
\]

The resulting theorem is

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\left(
|f_n(t)-f(t)|
+
|K_n(t)-K(t)|
+
|(y-f_n(t))^2-e(t)^2|
\right)
\xrightarrow{\mathbb P}0.
\]

## Why this does not yet prove width-first actual GD

Actual Euler updates \(u\), not \(\rho\):

\[
u^+=u+h\frac{Q}{1+u^2}.
\]

Transforming this exact discrete update gives

\[
\rho^+
=
\rho+hQ
+
h^2\frac{uQ^2}{(1+u^2)^2}
+
\frac{h^3Q^3}{3(1+u^2)^3}.
\]

The auxiliary MFP scheme uses only \(\rho^+=\rho+hQ\). At finite width the additional terms vanish in the ordinary mesh limit. After width is taken first, proving their accumulated disappearance requires mesh-uniform reachable \(L^4/L^6\) control of \(Q\), or an equivalent response-tail theorem. The existing square-\(L^2\) uniform-integrability proof does not supply that.

So the final answer is:

- The repaired arctangent proof is valid for the width limit of the exact finite-width continuous gradient flow.
- The old kernel proof contained a real gap, but that gap was repaired.
- The stronger width-first-actual-discrete-GD mesh theorem remains open.
- The MFP reconstruction above proves the valid theorem, conditional only on the fixed finite two-sided MFP master law. A fully self-contained MFP proof would additionally need to reprove its adaptive Gaussian-conditioning and singular-core theorem.

The full reconstruction is in [MFP_RECONSTRUCTION.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/temporary_arctan_mfp_reconstruction/MFP_RECONSTRUCTION.md). The current source theorem is [ARCTAN_THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md](/home/amir/Codes/PDE/studies/mean_field_peeling/nonlinear_activation_operator_ide/ARCTAN_THEOREM_AND_PROOF.md).

Qualified yes: the simultaneous diagonal limit is valid, but the displayed argument needs several corrections. It does not prove the original width-first limit, nor does it reproduce the earlier arctan initialization.

| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| Finite-width equations | Correct |
| Natural coordinate \(X=A+A^3/3\) | Correct |
| Joint scaling \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) | Works |
| Same initialization as the earlier theorem | False |
| Iterated limit \(n\to\infty\) first, then \(\eta\to0\) | Not proved |
| All parameter blocks active at \(t=0\) | False |
| Complete width-limit justification as written | Needs repair |

The decisive calculation supporting the joint limit is sound. If

\[
R=-2eB^*D,\qquad q(a)=\frac1{1+a^2},
\]

then exact GD gives

\[
A^+=A+\eta q(A)R
\]

and therefore exactly

\[
X^+
=
X+\eta R
+\eta^2Aq(A)^2R^2
+\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(A)^3R^3.
\]

Since

\[
\sup_a |a q(a)^2|=\frac9{16\sqrt3},
\qquad
\|R\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n,
\]

the defect from Euler evolution in \(X\) satisfies

\[
\|\rho_k\|_n
\le
\frac9{16\sqrt3}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R_k\|_n^2
+\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R_k\|_n^3.
\]

The required uniform bound on \(R_k\) can be proved explicitly. Writing \(a=\pi/2\),

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_n
\le
(1+2a^2\eta)\|C_k\|_n+2a\eta,
\]

and discrete Grönwall then bounds \(\|C_k\|_n\). Direct summation subsequently bounds \(\|C_k\|_\infty\) and \(\|B_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\), while

\[
\|R_k\|_n
\le
2|e_k|\,\|B_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|C_k\|_n .
\]

Consequently, on every compact macroscopic interval,

\[
\sup_{k\eta\le T}
d_n\!\left(\theta^{\mathrm{GD}}_{n,k},
\theta^{\mathrm{flow}}_n(k\eta)\right)
\le
C_T\bigl(\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n\bigr).
\]

Hence \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) works. In fact, this part works whenever

\[
\eta_n\sqrt n\longrightarrow0.
\]

There are nevertheless four material qualifications.

1. This is a different initialization.

Because \(C=nW^{(3)}\),

\[
\operatorname{Var}(C_{0,j})
=n^2\operatorname{Var}(W^{(3)}_{0,j})
=n^{-2},
\]

so

\[
C_0\longrightarrow0.
\]

The earlier repo theorem has an \(O(1)\) Gaussian normalized readout, corresponding to raw top-weight variance \(n^{-2}\), not \(n^{-4}\). The new zero-readout model is analytically easier.

2. The result is a simultaneous diagonal limit:

\[
n\to\infty,\qquad
\eta_n=n^{-2},\qquad
k_n\eta_n\to t.
\]

It does not establish

\[
\lim_{\eta\to0}\left[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\mathrm{GD}_{n,\eta}^{\lfloor t/\eta\rfloor}
\right].
\]

Thus it does not resolve the width-first fixed-\(\eta\) problem discussed earlier.

3. The width argument requires the repaired OMFP source theorem.

The reused \(B_0,B_0^*\) programs have singular Gram matrices because \(C_0=D_0=0\). Yang–Hu Theorem 7.4 by itself is not a sufficient citation unless its singular-core extension is supplied. One must use the repaired two-sided source construction, including null-space preservation and the exact adjoint identity.

Likewise, \(B_n\) must not be claimed to converge across widths in operator norm. Write

\[
B=\Gamma+Q
\]

with immutable Gaussian action \(\Gamma\), and compare the learned part \(Q\) in trace norm/action-law topology. Operator norm is only a uniform size bound.

4. At \(t=0\),

\[
K_3(0)>0,\qquad K_1(0)=K_2(0)=0.
\]

The hidden blocks become active at order \(t^2\):

\[
K_2(t)
=
4t^2\mathbb E[H_0^2]\mathbb E[U^2]+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K_1(t)
=
4t^2\mathbb E[q(A_0)^2\Xi^2]+o(t^2)>0,
\]

where

\[
\Xi=\mathbb E[U'(Z)]H_0+\sqrt{\mathbb E U^2}\,\Gamma_0.
\]

So the correct final verdict is:

\[
\boxed{\text{The altered zero-readout joint limit exists, after the stated repairs.}}
\]

But:

\[
\boxed{\text{The displayed argument is neither the original arctan theorem nor a width-first proof.}}
\]

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 21818 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.272Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes. The task has an affirmative solution. A convenient choice is a bounded nonlinear activation with a natural coordinate that regularizes the first-layer equation.

## Theorem

Take

\[
\phi(x)=\arctan x,
\]

\[
\sigma_{1,n}^{2}=1,\qquad
\sigma_{2,n}^{2}=\frac1n,\qquad
\sigma_{3,n}^{2}=\frac1{n^{4}},
\]

and

\[
\lambda_n^{(1)}=n,\qquad
\lambda_n^{(2)}=1,\qquad
\lambda_n^{(3)}=\frac1n,\qquad
\eta_{0,n}=\frac1{n^{2}}.
\]

Then

\[
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(1)}=n^{-1},\quad
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(2)}=n^{-2},\quad
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(3)}=n^{-3},
\]

so all raw layerwise steps vanish.

There is a deterministic \(T_*>0\) and an autonomous pointed-action mean-field gradient flow such that, for every \(0<T\le T_*\), the exact GD interpolation converges in probability along the full sequence \(n\to\infty\), uniformly on \([0,T]\). It satisfies all seven requested properties.

The limiting state is not determined by ordinary neuron marginals or an ordinary empirical edge law. It contains one immutable two-sided Gaussian action operator and its current learned trace-class perturbation. This is necessary because reused \(W^{(2)}\) and \(W^{(2)\top}\) create order-one response terms invisible to ordinary edge laws.

## 1. Exact finite-width dynamics

Use

\[
\langle u,v\rangle_n=\frac1n u^\top v,\qquad
u\otimes_n v=\frac1nuv^\top.
\]

Set

\[
u=W^{(1)},\qquad M=W^{(2)},\qquad c=nW^{(3)},
\]

and write

\[
h=\phi(u),\qquad s=Mh,\qquad g=\phi(s),
\]

\[
q(x)=\phi'(x)=\frac1{1+x^2},\qquad
d=c\odot q(s),
\]

\[
f_n=\langle c,g\rangle_n,\qquad e=f_n-1.
\]

Since \(W^{(3)}=c/n\), this is exactly the original predictor.

Direct differentiation gives

\[
\nabla_u f_n
=
\frac1n q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\]

\[
\nabla_M f_n
=
d\otimes_n h,
\qquad
\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f_n=g.
\]

Therefore exact GD is

\[
u^+
=
u-2\eta e\,q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\]

\[
M^+
=
M-2\eta e\,d\otimes_n h,
\]

\[
c^+
=
c-2\eta e\,g,
\tag{1}
\]

where \(\eta=\eta_{0,n}\).

The three kernel blocks are exactly

\[
K_n^{(1)}
=
\left\|q(u)\odot M^\top d\right\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(2)}
=
\|h\|_n^2\|d\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(3)}
=
\|g\|_n^2.
\tag{2}
\]

For the associated finite-width gradient flow,

\[
\dot f_n=-2eK_n,
\qquad
\dot L_n=-4e^2K_n,
\qquad
K_n=\sum_{\ell=1}^3K_n^{(\ell)}.
\tag{3}
\]

The hidden velocities are

\[
\dot u=-2e\,q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\qquad
V_n^{(1)2}=4e^2K_n^{(1)},
\tag{4}
\]

and

\[
\dot s
=
-2e
\left[
\|h\|_n^2d+
M\!\left(q(u)^2\odot M^\top d\right)
\right].
\tag{5}
\]

Equation (5) retains the reused-matrix response; replacing
\(Mq(u)^2M^\top\) by a scalar multiple of the identity would be incorrect.

## 2. The natural coordinate

Define

\[
\Theta(a)=a+\frac{a^3}{3},
\qquad
x=\Theta(u),
\qquad
J=\Theta^{-1}.
\]

Since

\[
\Theta'(a)=1+a^2=\frac1{q(a)},
\]

the gradient-flow equation becomes

\[
\dot x=-2eM^\top d.
\tag{6}
\]

Also,

\[
u=J(x),\qquad
h=\Psi(x):=\arctan J(x),
\]

with

\[
|J'(x)|=q(J(x))\le1,
\qquad
\Psi'(x)=q(J(x))^2\le1.
\tag{7}
\]

Thus \(J,\Psi\) are globally Lipschitz. This removes the uncontrolled product that would arise when trying to prove ambient-\(L^2\) Lipschitzness directly in \(u\).

Write

\[
M=\Gamma_n+Q_n,\qquad \Gamma_n=M(0).
\]

Then

\[
\dot Q_n=-2e\,d\otimes_n h.
\tag{8}
\]

The learned part satisfies

\[
\|d\otimes_n h\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
=
\|d\|_n\|h\|_n,
\]

so \(Q_n\) is naturally controlled in trace norm.

## 3. The required Gaussian-action source

We use the following specialization of the two-sided Tensor Program Master Theorem.

> Let \(\Gamma_n\) have independent \(N(0,1/n)\) entries, independently of finitely many iid seed vectors with moments of every order. Fix a finite program formed from \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\), coordinatewise pseudo-Lipschitz maps, finite linear combinations, and normalized empirical moments. Then all finite same-side joint empirical laws and all scalar moments converge almost surely to deterministic master laws. The conclusion permits repeated use of \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\) and singular limiting Gram matrices.

This is the precise content needed from the Tensor Program Master Theorem, [Yang–Hu, Theorem G.4 and Assumption L.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), based on the extended two-sided theorem in [Tensor Programs III](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.10685).

Its hypotheses hold here:

- \(\Gamma_n\) is iid \(N(0,1/n)\).
- The initial seed \(x_0=\Theta(u_0)\) is a polynomial Gaussian map.
- \(J,\Psi,q,\arctan\) are globally Lipschitz.
- Products and moment maps are pseudo-Lipschitz with polynomial growth.
- At a fixed auxiliary Euler mesh, only finitely many operations occur.
- Reuse of \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\) is explicitly allowed.
- The zero limiting readout creates singular Grams, but the cited pseudo-Lipschitz version does not require rank stability.

For completeness, the source has a genuine bounded operator realization. A \(1/4\)-net argument gives

\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}>10)
\le 2e^{-8n}.
\tag{9}
\]

Enumerate a countable rational family of all finite two-sided probe programs. Their limiting inner products define two pre-Hilbert spaces. Quotient by zero norm and complete to obtain

\[
\mathcal H_R=L^2(\Omega_R),\qquad
\mathcal H_L=L^2(\Omega_L).
\]

The bound (9) implies

\[
\|\Gamma v\|_{\mathcal H_L}
\le10\|v\|_{\mathcal H_R}.
\]

Finite-dimensional duality,

\[
\langle \Gamma_nv,w\rangle_n
=
\langle v,\Gamma_n^\top w\rangle_n,
\]

passes to the limit, so the reverse action is the genuine Hilbert adjoint \(\Gamma^*\).

This pointed Gaussian action is deterministic as a law. It retains neither a finite matrix nor a training history.

A convergence-determining state metric is obtained by enumerating all finite current probes \(\mathbf P_r\) and setting

\[
d_{\mathrm{act}}(\mathsf S,\widetilde{\mathsf S})
=
\sum_{r\ge1}2^{-r}
\left(
1\wedge
W_2\bigl(
\operatorname{Law}\mathbf P_r(\mathsf S),
\operatorname{Law}\mathbf P_r(\widetilde{\mathsf S})
\bigr)
\right).
\tag{10}
\]

It detects, in particular, both \(Mv\) and \(M^*w\), all empirical Grams, and every vector appearing in (1)–(5).

## 4. Autonomous limiting gradient flow

On the pointed source \((\mathcal H_R,\mathcal H_L,\Gamma,\Gamma^*)\), initialize

\[
U_0\sim N(0,1),\qquad
X_0=\Theta(U_0),\qquad
C_0=0,\qquad
Q_0=0.
\]

For the current state \((X,C,Q)\), define

\[
U=J(X),\qquad H=\arctan U,
\]

\[
B=\Gamma+Q,\qquad S=BH,
\]

\[
G=\arctan S,\qquad
D=Cq(S),
\]

\[
F=\langle C,G\rangle_L,\qquad e=F-1.
\]

The limiting IDE is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\dot X&=-2eB^*D,\\
\dot C&=-2eG,\\
\dot Q&=-2e\,D\otimes H.
\end{aligned}}
\tag{11}
\]

Here

\[
(D\otimes H)v=D\langle H,v\rangle_R.
\]

This is current-state, autonomous, and restartable: at time \(\tau\), the state

\[
(\Gamma;X_\tau,C_\tau,Q_\tau)
\]

is sufficient to continue (11). No past trajectory is needed.

### Well-posedness

Put \(a=\pi/2\). From the gradient identity,

\[
\dot e=-2eK,
\tag{12}
\]

where

\[
K^{(1)}=\|q(U)B^*D\|_R^2,
\]

\[
K^{(2)}=\|H\|_R^2\|D\|_L^2,
\qquad
K^{(3)}=\|G\|_L^2.
\tag{13}
\]

Since \(e(0)=-1\),

\[
|e(t)|\le1.
\tag{14}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty
\le2a t=\pi t,
\qquad
\|D(t)\|_2\le\pi t.
\tag{15}
\]

Furthermore,

\[
\|\dot Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le2\|D(t)\|_2\|H(t)\|_2
\le \pi^2t,
\]

and hence

\[
\|Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le\frac{\pi^2t^2}{2}.
\tag{16}
\]

Finally,

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le2\bigl(\|\Gamma\|+\|Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\bigr)\|D(t)\|_2.
\tag{17}
\]

These bounds rule out finite-time blowup.

For two states satisfying fixed versions of (15)–(17),

\[
\|H-\widetilde H\|_2\le\|X-\widetilde X\|_2,
\]

\[
\|S-\widetilde S\|_2
\le
\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|X-\widetilde X\|_2
+a\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1},
\]

and

\[
\|D-\widetilde D\|_2
\le
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2
+
\|\widetilde C\|_\infty\|q'\|_\infty
\|S-\widetilde S\|_2.
\tag{18}
\]

The rank-one estimate

\[
\|D\otimes H-\widetilde D\otimes\widetilde H\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
\|D-\widetilde D\|_2\|H\|_2
+
\|\widetilde D\|_2\|H-\widetilde H\|_2
\tag{19}
\]

then proves local Lipschitzness in

\[
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\]

Picard iteration, with the pointwise envelope (15), gives local existence and uniqueness; (14)–(17) extend it to every finite time. Thus (11) defines a semigroup.

## 5. Width identification

Fix an auxiliary mesh \(\delta>0\). Euler iteration of (11) gives

\[
Q_k
=
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\otimes H_r.
\]

Therefore, for every vector \(v\),

\[
B_kv
=
\Gamma v
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_rD_r\langle H_r,v\rangle,
\tag{20}
\]

and

\[
B_k^*w
=
\Gamma^*w
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}
e_rH_r\langle D_r,w\rangle.
\tag{21}
\]

For fixed \(T/\delta\), equations (20)–(21) are a finite two-sided Gaussian program. The Master Theorem therefore identifies every probe, empirical Gram, output, kernel block, and velocity field at that mesh.

On the event \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le10\), estimates (15)–(19) hold uniformly in \(n\). The ordinary Euler calculation gives

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\left[
\|X_n(t)-X_n^\delta(t)\|_n
+
\|C_n(t)-C_n^\delta(t)\|_n
+
\|Q_n(t)-Q_n^\delta(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\right]
\le C_T\delta.
\tag{22}
\]

The same estimate holds for the limiting IDE.

Taking \(n\to\infty\) at fixed \(\delta\), and then \(\delta\downarrow0\), proves

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
d_{\mathrm{act}}(\mathsf S_n^{\mathrm{flow}}(t),\mathsf S(t))
\xrightarrow{\mathbb P}0.
\tag{23}
\]

This is full-sequence convergence: fixed-mesh limits are deterministic, and uniqueness of (11) removes subsequence choices.

The actual normalized readout initialization satisfies

\[
C_{0,j}=nW_j^{(3)}(0)\sim N(0,n^{-2}),
\]

so

\[
\|C_0\|_n\to0,
\qquad
\|C_0\|_\infty\to0
\quad\text{in probability}.
\tag{24}
\]

The stability estimate (18) shows that replacing it by the limiting value \(C_0=0\) changes the compact-time trajectory by \(o_{\mathbb P}(1)\).

## 6. The exact-GD diagonal

The transformed auxiliary Euler scheme is not exactly raw GD, so this bridge must be checked separately.

Let

\[
R=-2eM^\top d.
\]

From (1),

\[
u^+=u+\eta q(u)R.
\]

Because \(\Theta\) is cubic,

\[
\Theta(u^+)
=
\Theta(u)
+
\eta R
+
\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2
+
\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3
\tag{25}
\]

coordinatewise.

Now

\[
\sup_x |x|q(x)^2=\frac9{16\sqrt3},
\qquad
0<q\le1,
\qquad
\|R\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n.
\]

Hence the one-step defect satisfies

\[
\|\rho_k\|_n
\le
\frac9{16\sqrt3}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R_k\|_n^2
+
\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R_k\|_n^3.
\tag{26}
\]

The required uniform bound on \(R_k\) follows directly. If

\[
c_k=\|C_k\|_n,
\]

then, because \(\|G_k\|_n\le a\),

\[
c_{k+1}
\le
(1+2a^2\eta)c_k+2a\eta.
\tag{27}
\]

Discrete Grönwall bounds \(c_k\) for \(k\eta\le T\). Directly summing the updates then bounds

\[
\|C_k\|_\infty,\qquad
\|M_k\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\]

and

\[
\|R_k\|_n
\le
2|e_k|\|M_k\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|C_k\|_n
\le R_T
\tag{28}
\]

uniformly in \(n\).

The perturbed-Euler recursion obtained from (25), together with the local truncation error of the continuous ODE, therefore gives

\[
\sup_{k\eta\le T}
d_n(\mathsf S_{n,k}^{\mathrm{GD}},\mathsf S_n^{\mathrm{flow}}(k\eta))
\le
C_T\left(
\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n
\right),
\tag{29}
\]

where

\[
d_n=
\|\Delta X\|_n+
\|\Delta C\|_n+
\|\Delta Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\]

For \(\eta=n^{-2}\), the right side is

\[
O_T(n^{-2}+n^{-3/2}+n^{-3})\to0.
\]

Thus no growing-depth Tensor Program theorem is being assumed: the \(O(n^2)\) exact GD steps are compared directly with the finite-width ODE, and only the ODE is identified through a fixed auxiliary mesh.

Combining (23) and (29) proves full joint convergence of exact GD.

## 7. Path laws, uniform integrability, kernels, and velocities

At a fixed auxiliary mesh, every finite path tuple is a fixed Gaussian program. Applying the Master Theorem to fourth powers gives \(W_2\) convergence and square uniform integrability.

For mesh points \(t_k\), if

\[
\sup_k\|z_n(t_k)-z_n^\delta(t_k)\|_n\le\varepsilon_\delta,
\]

then Cauchy–Schwarz on each interval gives

\[
\frac1n\sum_i
\|z_{n,i}-z_{n,i}^\delta\|_\infty^2
\le
C\frac{T}{\delta}\varepsilon_\delta^2
+
C\delta
\int_0^T
\left(V_n^2+V_n^{\delta\,2}\right)\,dt.
\tag{30}
\]

For the auxiliary Euler approximation, \(\varepsilon_\delta=O(\delta)\), so the right side is \(O(\delta)\).

For exact GD, (29) gives
\(\varepsilon_n=O(\eta\sqrt n)\). Hence

\[
\frac{T}{\eta}\varepsilon_n^2
=
O(T\eta n)
=
O(T/n)\to0.
\]

Consequently, for \(\ell=1,2\),

\[
\overline\mu_n^{(\ell)}
\longrightarrow
\overline\mu^{(\ell)}
\quad\text{in }W_2(C([0,T])).
\tag{31}
\]

The square-tail transfer is explicit. For any coupled fields \(Y,Z\),

\[
|Y|^2\mathbf 1_{\{|Y|>R\}}
\le
2|Z|^2\mathbf 1_{\{|Z|>R/2\}}
+
4|Y-Z|^2.
\tag{32}
\]

Apply (32) first with the fixed-mesh field \(Z\), whose fourth moments converge, and then let \(\delta\downarrow0\). This establishes the required path-square uniform integrability.

The only weighted term needing extra care is

\[
q(U)B^*D.
\]

The fields \(B^*D\) have square-tail tightness by the same fixed-mesh argument. If \(X_n\to X\) and \(P_n=B_n^*D_n\to P\) in \(L^2\), then for every \(M\),

\[
\|(q(J(X_n))-q(J(X)))P\|_2^2
\le
C M^2\|X_n-X\|_2^2
+
4\mathbb E[P^2\mathbf1_{\{|P|>M\}}].
\tag{33}
\]

First let \(n\to\infty\), then \(M\to\infty\). This proves convergence of the first-layer kernel and velocity. Applying \(B\), whose operator norm is uniformly bounded, also passes the second-layer velocity term in (5).

Thus

\[
K_n\to K,\qquad
f_n\to F,\qquad
L_n\to e^2
\]

uniformly on compact intervals, while

\[
V_n^{(\ell)2}\to V^{(\ell)2}
\quad\text{in }L^1(0,T).
\tag{34}
\]

Every exact layer update is determined by the limiting pointed-action state.

## 8. Strict nondegeneracy

Let

\[
U_0\sim N(0,1),\qquad
H_0=\arctan U_0,
\qquad
m=\mathbb E H_0^2>0.
\]

The initial second preactivation is

\[
Z=\Gamma H_0\sim N(0,m).
\]

Define

\[
G=\arctan Z,
\qquad
Y=Gq(Z),
\qquad
\nu=\mathbb E Y^2>0,
\]

\[
\Xi=\Gamma^*Y,
\qquad
d_0=\|q(U_0)\Xi\|_2^2.
\]

The adjoint identity gives

\[
\langle H_0,\Xi\rangle
=
\langle Z,Y\rangle
=
\mathbb E\left[
\frac{Z\arctan Z}{1+Z^2}
\right]>0.
\]

Hence \(\Xi\ne0\). Since \(q(U_0)>0\) almost surely,

\[
d_0>0.
\tag{35}
\]

Let

\[
\mathcal R
=
mI+
\Gamma M_{q(U_0)^2}\Gamma^*.
\]

It is positive and

\[
\langle Y,\mathcal RY\rangle
=
m\nu+d_0.
\tag{36}
\]

Directly integrating (11) near zero gives, in the indicated norms,

\[
C(t)=2tG+o_{L^2}(t),
\qquad
D(t)=2tY+o_{L^2}(t),
\]

\[
X(t)=X_0+2t^2\Xi+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
Q(t)=2t^2Y\otimes H_0+o_{\mathfrak S_1}(t^2),
\]

\[
U(t)=U_0+2t^2q(U_0)\Xi+o_{L^2}(t^2),
\]

\[
S(t)=Z+2t^2\mathcal RY+o_{L^2}(t^2).
\tag{37}
\]

These follow from \(D(t)/t\to2Y\), bounded continuity of the coordinate maps, and the adjoint identity; no finite-width Taylor/width interchange is used.

Consequently,

\[
K^{(1)}(t)
=
4d_0t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K^{(2)}(t)
=
4m\nu t^2+o(t^2),
\]

and, putting

\[
k_3=\mathbb E(\arctan Z)^2>0,
\]

\[
K^{(3)}(t)
=
k_3+4(m\nu+d_0)t^2+o(t^2).
\]

Therefore

\[
K(t)
=
k_3+8(m\nu+d_0)t^2+o(t^2).
\tag{38}
\]

In particular, the kernel is nonconstant.

The feature velocities satisfy

\[
V^{(1)}(t)^2
=
16d_0t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
V^{(2)}(t)^2
=
16\|\mathcal RY\|_2^2t^2+o(t^2),
\tag{39}
\]

and \(\mathcal R\ge mI\), so the second coefficient is positive. Moreover,

\[
\mathbb E|U(t)-U_0|^2
=
4d_0t^4+o(t^4)>0,
\]

\[
\mathbb E|S(t)-Z|^2
=
4\|\mathcal RY\|_2^2t^4+o(t^4)>0.
\tag{40}
\]

Thus both hidden layers have genuine macroscopic feature motion.

At time zero,

\[
\operatorname{Var}(U_0)=1,
\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Z)=m>0.
\]

Their \(W_2\)-continuous laws retain finite strictly positive variance on a sufficiently short interval.

For any nondegenerate Gaussian \(Y_0\),

\[
\inf_{a,b}
\mathbb E|\arctan Y_0-aY_0-b|^2>0:
\]

otherwise continuity and full support would force \(\arctan x=ax+b\) for every \(x\), which is false. The regression residual is continuous under \(W_2\) while the variance is bounded away from zero, since it is determined by

\[
\mathbb EY,\quad
\mathbb EY^2,\quad
\mathbb E\phi(Y),\quad
\mathbb EY\phi(Y),\quad
\mathbb E\phi(Y)^2.
\]

Hence both nonlinear residuals remain positive on a short interval.

Finally,

\[
F(0)=0\ne1,
\qquad
\dot F(0)=2k_3>0,
\]

and

\[
\dot L(0)=-4k_3<0.
\tag{41}
\]

Choose \(T_*>0\) small enough that the two variances and nonlinear residuals remain positive and the positive coefficients in (38)–(40) dominate their remainders. Then every \(0<T\le T_*\) satisfies all seven requested conditions.

A useful adversarial check is that ordinary edge laws cannot replace the pointed action state. If \(w\perp h\), \(\|w\|_n=1\), and

\[
R=\gamma\,d\otimes_n w,
\]

then \(Rh=0\), so the current forward preactivations and node laws are unchanged, while

\[
R^\top d
=
\gamma w\|d\|_n^2
\]

changes the first-layer velocity by order one. The pointed-action topology detects this through the probe \(M^\top d\); an ordinary marginal or edge empirical law does not. This is why the enriched—but autonomous and current-state—mean-field state is essential.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 21853 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.273Z | assistant | final_answer ===
Yes. A fully joint nonlinear construction exists. The workable activation is not \(z^2\), but

\[
\phi(z)=\arctan z.
\]

Choose

\[
\sigma_{1,n}^2=1,\qquad
\sigma_{2,n}^2=\frac1n,\qquad
\sigma_{3,n}^2=\frac1{n^4},
\]

\[
\lambda_n^{(1)}=n,\qquad
\lambda_n^{(2)}=1,\qquad
\lambda_n^{(3)}=\frac1n,
\qquad
\eta_{0,n}=\frac1{n^2}.
\]

Then

\[
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(1)}=\frac1n,\quad
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(2)}=\frac1{n^2},\quad
\eta_{0,n}\lambda_n^{(3)}=\frac1{n^3},
\]

so all raw layerwise steps vanish.

The result below holds for the usual polygonal interpolation of the parameters. Piecewise-constant interpolation gives the same limit.

## Theorem

For the activation and scaling above, there is a deterministic, autonomous, restartable pointed-action mean-field flow. The exact interpolated GD trajectories converge to it in probability, along the full sequence \(n\to\infty\), uniformly on every finite time interval in a topology that determines all forward fields, backward fields, layer updates, kernel blocks, output, loss, and both hidden path laws.

The flow exists globally. Moreover, there is \(T_*>0\) such that, for every \(0<T\le T_*\), all seven properties in the question hold.

## 1. Exact finite-width equations

Write

\[
u=W^{(1)},\qquad M=W^{(2)},\qquad c=nW^{(3)}
\]

and use

\[
\langle v,w\rangle_n=\frac1n v^\top w,\qquad
\|v\|_n^2=\langle v,v\rangle_n.
\]

Set

\[
h=\phi(u),\qquad s=Mh,\qquad g=\phi(s),
\]

\[
q(x)=\phi'(x)=\frac1{1+x^2},\qquad
d=c\odot q(s),
\]

\[
f_n=\langle c,g\rangle_n,\qquad e=f_n-1.
\]

For vectors \(a,b\), define the normalized rank-one map

\[
a\otimes_n b
=
\frac1n ab^\top,
\qquad
(a\otimes_n b)v=a\langle b,v\rangle_n.
\]

Direct differentiation gives

\[
\nabla_u f_n
=
\frac1n q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\]

\[
\nabla_M f_n
=
\frac1n dh^\top,
\qquad
\nabla_{W^{(3)}}f_n=g.
\]

Hence exact GD is

\[
u^+=u-2\eta e\,q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\]

\[
M^+=M-2\eta e\,d\otimes_n h,
\]

\[
c^+=c-2\eta e\,g,
\tag{1}
\]

where \(\eta=\eta_{0,n}\).

The three kernel blocks are exactly

\[
K_n^{(1)}
=
\left\|q(u)\odot M^\top d\right\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(2)}
=
\|h\|_n^2\|d\|_n^2,
\]

\[
K_n^{(3)}
=
\|g\|_n^2.
\tag{2}
\]

For the corresponding finite-width continuous gradient flow,

\[
\dot f_n=-2eK_n,\qquad
\dot L_n=-4e^2K_n,
\qquad
K_n=\sum_{\ell=1}^3K_n^{(\ell)}.
\tag{3}
\]

The hidden velocities are

\[
\dot u=-2e\,q(u)\odot M^\top d,
\]

and

\[
\dot s
=
-2e\left[
\|h\|_n^2d+
M\!\left(q(u)^2\odot M^\top d\right)
\right].
\tag{4}
\]

Thus

\[
V_n^{(1)2}=4e^2K_n^{(1)}.
\tag{5}
\]

## 2. Why ordinary neuron or edge laws do not close

The middle matrix must be retained through its action, not through its entry distribution alone.

For example, at a current state choose \(w\) with

\[
\langle w,h\rangle_n=0,\qquad \|w\|_n=1,
\]

and define

\[
R=\gamma\,d\otimes_n w.
\]

Then

\[
(M+R)h=Mh,
\]

so the current forward preactivations, output, \(K^{(2)}\), and \(K^{(3)}\) do not change. The ordinary empirical edge perturbation vanishes:

\[
\frac1{n^2}\sum_{ji}
\bigl(\sqrt n\,R_{ji}\bigr)^2
=
\frac{\gamma^2\|d\|_n^2}{n}\longrightarrow0.
\]

Nevertheless,

\[
(M+R)^\top d-M^\top d
=
\gamma\|d\|_n^2w,
\]

which changes the first-layer velocity and \(K^{(1)}\) at order one.

Consequently, no closure involving only hidden-node laws or the ordinary empirical edge law can satisfy property 2. The correct state is the current two-sided action law of \(M,M^\top\).

## 3. Natural coordinate and the limiting state

Define

\[
\Theta(a)=a+\frac{a^3}{3},\qquad x=\Theta(u),
\]

and let \(J=\Theta^{-1}\). Since

\[
\Theta'(a)=1+a^2=q(a)^{-1},
\]

the continuous equation for \(x\) is

\[
\dot x=-2eM^\top d.
\tag{6}
\]

Moreover, \(J\) is globally \(1\)-Lipschitz, and

\[
\Psi(x):=\arctan J(x)
\]

is bounded and globally Lipschitz, with

\[
\Psi'(x)=q(J(x))^2.
\tag{7}
\]

Write

\[
M_0=\Gamma_n,\qquad Q_n=M-\Gamma_n,
\]

where \((\Gamma_n)_{ji}\sim N(0,1/n)\). The learned part satisfies

\[
\dot Q_n=-2e\,d\otimes_n h.
\tag{8}
\]

Because

\[
\|a\otimes_n b\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
=
\|a\|_n\|b\|_n,
\]

\(Q_n\) is uniformly trace-class on compact time intervals.

### The fixed-program theorem actually used

The only external probabilistic theorem used is the following fixed-finite-program result.

Consider a fixed finite program generated from independent coordinate seeds and iid Gaussian matrices with entries \(N(0,1/n)\), using:

- finitely many \(Gv\) and \(G^\top w\) operations, with matrix reuse allowed;
- coordinatewise pseudo-Lipschitz maps;
- empirical moments and continuous scalar operations.

Then, for every fixed collection of generated vectors and every pseudo-Lipschitz test function, the empirical average converges almost surely to the deterministic Tensor-Program master law. Scalar program variables converge as well. This is precisely [Yang–Hu, Theorem G.4 under Assumption L.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), referring to the two-sided master theorem in [Tensor Programs III](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.10685).

The theorem is only a fixed-program theorem; it is not invoked with \(n\)-dependent training depth.

Its assumptions hold here:

- \(u_0\) is iid Gaussian and \(\Theta(u_0)\) is a polynomial Gaussian coordinate map.
- \(J,\Psi,q,\arctan\) are globally Lipschitz or polynomially bounded.
- Products and scalar multiplications are pseudo-Lipschitz of finite degree.
- Every auxiliary Euler mesh below has a fixed number \(T/\delta\) of steps when \(n\to\infty\).
- Reuse of both \(\Gamma_n\) and \(\Gamma_n^\top\) is part of the theorem.
- The pseudo-Lipschitz version of the theorem imposes no separate rank-stability assumption, so zero fields and singular limiting Gram matrices are permitted.

Applying the theorem to \(|z|^p\), for each fixed \(p\), also gives convergence of all required fixed-program moments.

### Pointed Gaussian action

Enumerate a countable rational program language generated from the right seed \(u_0\), constants, \(\Gamma_n,\Gamma_n^\top\), coordinate maps, and empirical inner products. The master theorem gives consistent deterministic finite-dimensional laws.

Quotient the right and left program fields by their limiting zero \(L^2\)-norms and complete them. This produces two probability \(L^2\)-spaces

\[
\mathcal H_R,\qquad \mathcal H_L.
\]

A \(1/4\)-net argument gives, for some numerical \(c>0\),

\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}>10)\le 2e^{-cn}.
\]

Therefore, for every formal right probe \(v\),

\[
\|\Gamma v\|_{\mathcal H_L}
\le 10\|v\|_{\mathcal H_R}.
\]

Thus the matrix action descends to a genuine bounded operator

\[
\Gamma:\mathcal H_R\to\mathcal H_L.
\]

Finite-dimensional duality,

\[
\langle \Gamma_nv,w\rangle_n
=
\langle v,\Gamma_n^\top w\rangle_n,
\]

passes to the limit and proves that the reverse action is the Hilbert adjoint \(\Gamma^*\).

This pointed action is deterministic and width-independent. It retains neither a finite matrix nor training history.

## 4. Autonomous limiting IDE

On this pointed action space, initialize

\[
U_0\sim N(0,1),\qquad
X_0=\Theta(U_0),\qquad
C_0=0,\qquad
Q_0=0.
\]

At time \(t\), define

\[
U=J(X),\qquad H=\arctan U,
\]

\[
M=\Gamma+Q,\qquad S=MH,
\]

\[
G=\arctan S,\qquad
D=Cq(S),
\]

\[
F=\mathbb E_L[CG],\qquad e=F-1.
\]

The limit is

\[
\dot X=-2eM^*D,
\]

\[
\dot C=-2eG,
\]

\[
\dot Q=-2eD\otimes H.
\tag{9}
\]

Here \(D\otimes H:v\mapsto D\,\mathbb E_R[Hv]\).

Equation (9) is autonomous. At any restart time \(\tau\), the current state

\[
(\Gamma;X_\tau,C_\tau,Q_\tau)
\]

is sufficient to continue the same equation. The entire learned middle-layer history is compressed into the single current trace-class operator \(Q_\tau\).

### Well-posedness

Let \(a=\pi/2\). The vector field is locally Lipschitz in

\[
\|X-\widetilde X\|_2+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\tag{10}
\]

on sets with bounded \(\|C\|_\infty\), \(\|Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\).

Indeed,

\[
\|H-\widetilde H\|_2
\le\|X-\widetilde X\|_2,
\]

\[
\|S-\widetilde S\|_2
\le
\|M\|_{\rm op}\|H-\widetilde H\|_2
+a\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1},
\]

and

\[
\|D-\widetilde D\|_2
\le
\|C-\widetilde C\|_2+
\|\widetilde C\|_\infty
\|q(S)-q(\widetilde S)\|_2.
\]

The rank-one estimate

\[
\|D\otimes H-\widetilde D\otimes\widetilde H\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
\|D-\widetilde D\|_2\|H\|_2
+
\|\widetilde D\|_2\|H-\widetilde H\|_2
\]

closes the estimate. Picard iterates start from \(C_0=0\) and satisfy a common \(L^\infty\) envelope, so the contraction may be performed in the metric (10).

Differentiating \(F\) gives

\[
\dot F=-2eK,
\tag{11}
\]

where

\[
K^{(1)}=\|q(U)M^*D\|_2^2,
\]

\[
K^{(2)}=\|H\|_2^2\|D\|_2^2,
\]

\[
K^{(3)}=\|G\|_2^2.
\tag{12}
\]

Thus

\[
e(t)
=
-\exp\left(-2\int_0^tK(s)\,ds\right),
\]

so \(|e(t)|\le1\). Consequently,

\[
\|C(t)\|_\infty
\le
2a t=\pi t,
\]

\[
\|\dot Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\le
2\|D(t)\|_2\|H(t)\|_2
\le \pi^2t,
\]

and

\[
\|\dot X(t)\|_2
\le
2\bigl(\|\Gamma\|+\|Q(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}\bigr)\|D(t)\|_2.
\]

These exclude finite-time blowup. Hence (9) has a unique global solution.

## 5. Width limit without a growing-depth Tensor-Program invocation

Fix an auxiliary mesh \(\delta>0\). Explicit Euler for (9) gives

\[
Q_k
=
-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\otimes H_r.
\]

Therefore, for any vector \(v\),

\[
(\Gamma+Q_k)v
=
\Gamma v
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\langle H_r,v\rangle,
\tag{13}
\]

and similarly

\[
(\Gamma^*+Q_k^*)w
=
\Gamma^*w
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rH_r\langle D_r,w\rangle.
\tag{14}
\]

For fixed \(T,\delta\), equations (13)–(14) make the entire \(T/\delta\)-step Euler recursion a fixed finite two-sided Gaussian program. The external master theorem therefore applies exactly here.

On the event \(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\le10\), the finite-width and limiting vector fields have the same compact-time Lipschitz bound. Directly from

\[
Y(t_{k+1})-Y(t_k)-\delta\mathcal F(Y(t_k))
=
\int_{t_k}^{t_{k+1}}
[\mathcal F(Y(s))-\mathcal F(Y(t_k))]\,ds,
\]

one obtains

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
\left(
\|X_n(t)-X_n^\delta(t)\|_n+
\|C_n(t)-C_n^\delta(t)\|_n+
\|Q_n(t)-Q_n^\delta(t)\|_{\mathfrak S_1}
\right)
\le C_T\delta.
\tag{15}
\]

The same estimate holds in the limiting action space.

Thus the order of argument is:

1. fix \(\delta\);
2. apply the fixed finite-program theorem as \(n\to\infty\);
3. use (15) to send \(\delta\downarrow0\).

No Tensor-Program theorem is applied to a number of steps growing with \(n\).

The actual initialization has

\[
C_{0,j}=nW_j^{(3)}(0)\sim N(0,n^{-2}),
\]

so

\[
\mathbb E\|C_0\|_n^2=n^{-2},
\qquad
\|C_0\|_\infty\longrightarrow0
\]

in probability. Stability of (9) therefore permits replacing \(C_{0,n}\) by the limiting field \(C_0=0\).

## 6. Exact GD versus the finite-width flow

The transformed \(X\)-update is not exactly Euler, so this bridge must be checked separately.

Put

\[
R=-2eM^\top D.
\]

Then (1) says

\[
u^+=u+\eta q(u)R.
\]

Because \(\Theta\) is cubic,

\[
X^+
=
X+\eta R+
\eta^2u q(u)^2R^2+
\frac{\eta^3}{3}q(u)^3R^3.
\tag{16}
\]

This identity is exact.

Moreover,

\[
\sup_u |u|q(u)^2=\frac9{16\sqrt3},
\qquad
0<q(u)\le1,
\]

and

\[
\|R\|_\infty\le\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n.
\]

Hence the one-step coordinate defect \(\zeta\) satisfies

\[
\|\zeta\|_n
\le
\frac9{16\sqrt3}\eta^2\sqrt n\,\|R\|_n^2
+
\frac13\eta^3n\,\|R\|_n^3.
\tag{17}
\]

Uniform bounds needed in (17) follow directly from the exact discrete equations. Since \(\|G\|_n\le a\),

\[
\|C_{k+1}\|_n
\le
(1+2a^2\eta)\|C_k\|_n+2a\eta.
\tag{18}
\]

Discrete Grönwall bounds \(\|C_k\|_n\). Direct summation then bounds \(\|C_k\|_\infty\) and \(\|M_k\|_{\rm op}\), while

\[
\|R_k\|_n
\le
2|e_k|\|M_k\|_{\rm op}\|C_k\|_n.
\]

All are bounded by a width-independent \(C_T\) for \(k\eta\le T\), on the high-probability initialization event.

A perturbed-Euler Grönwall estimate applied to (16) gives

\[
\sup_{k\eta\le T}
d_n\!\left(
Y^{\rm GD}_{n,k},Y_n^{\rm flow}(k\eta)
\right)
\le
C_T\left(
\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n
\right),
\tag{19}
\]

where

\[
d_n(Y,\widetilde Y)
=
\|X-\widetilde X\|_n+
\|C-\widetilde C\|_n+
\|Q-\widetilde Q\|_{\mathfrak S_1}.
\]

For \(\eta=n^{-2}\),

\[
\eta+\eta\sqrt n+\eta^2n
=
n^{-2}+n^{-3/2}+n^{-3}
\longrightarrow0.
\]

This proves the genuine joint diagonal limit; it is not a composition of two uncontrolled iterated limits.

## 7. Action topology, path laws, and uniform integrability

Enumerate all finite typed probes generated from the current state using:

- coordinate maps;
- scalar products;
- \(M,M^*\);
- finite-rank current updates;
- sums and products.

For a finite tuple \(P_r\) of same-side probes, let \(\mu_{P_r}\) be its empirical joint law. Define

\[
d_{\rm act}(\mathsf S,\widetilde{\mathsf S})
=
\sum_{r\ge1}2^{-r}
\left(
1\wedge W_2(\mu_{P_r},\widetilde\mu_{P_r})
\right).
\tag{20}
\]

This is convergence-determining for every field appearing in the vector field, including the otherwise invisible field \(M^*D\).

For fixed auxiliary mesh, the master theorem gives convergence of every finite collection of probes and every \(p\)-moment. Estimate (15), followed by a finite-probe truncation of the sum (20), gives

\[
\sup_{t\le T}
d_{\rm act}(\mathsf S_n^{\rm flow}(t),\mathsf S(t))
\longrightarrow0
\]

in probability. Equation (19) transfers this to exact GD.

For the hidden path laws, the elementary inequality

\[
\frac1n\sum_i
\sup_{s,t\in I}|z_i(t)-z_i(s)|^2
\le
|I|\int_I V_n(t)^2\,dt
\tag{21}
\]

provides temporal tightness. If two paths have mesh-point \(L^2\)-error at most \(\varepsilon\) on a mesh of size \(h\), then

\[
\frac1n\sum_i
\|z_i-\widetilde z_i\|_\infty^2
\le
2\left(\frac{T}{h}+1\right)\varepsilon^2
+
4h\int_0^T(V_n^2+\widetilde V_n^2)\,dt.
\tag{22}
\]

For flow versus fixed-mesh Euler, \(\varepsilon=O(h)\), so the right side is \(O(h)\). For exact GD versus flow,

\[
h=\eta=n^{-2},
\qquad
\varepsilon=O(n^{-3/2}),
\]

and the right side is \(O(n^{-1})\).

At every fixed auxiliary mesh, the master theorem gives moments of order \(p>2\), hence square uniform integrability. It transfers to the exact paths through the following pointwise estimate. For coupled nonnegative path norms \(X,Y\),

\[
X^2\mathbf 1_{\{X>R\}}
\le
2Y^2\mathbf 1_{\{Y>R/2\}}
+
4(X-Y)^2.
\tag{23}
\]

First fix the auxiliary mesh, let \(R\to\infty\), and then let the mesh vanish. Equations (22)–(23) prove

\[
\overline\mu_n^{(\ell)}
\longrightarrow
\overline\mu^{(\ell)}
\quad\text{in }W_2(C([0,T])),
\qquad \ell=1,2.
\tag{24}
\]

The same approximation gives square-tail tightness for \(M^*D\). Therefore weighted products also pass. Explicitly, if \(P_n\to P\) in \(L^2\), \(X_n\to X\) in \(L^2\), and \(P_n^2\) is uniformly integrable, then, for any \(R>0\),

\[
\|(q(J(X_n))-q(J(X)))P\|_2^2
\le
C R^2\|X_n-X\|_2^2
+
4\mathbb E[P^2\mathbf 1_{\{|P|>R\}}].
\]

Let \(n\to\infty\), then \(R\to\infty\). This proves convergence of the first-layer velocity, \(K^{(1)}\), and the reused-adjoint term in \(\dot S\).

Consequently,

\[
V_n^{(\ell)2}\to V^{(\ell)2}
\quad\text{in }L^1(0,T),
\]

and

\[
K_n\to K,\qquad f_n\to F,\qquad L_n\to e^2
\]

uniformly on compact time intervals.

## 8. Strict nonlinear feature learning

It remains to prove that the limiting flow is not lazy or linear.

Let

\[
U_0\sim N(0,1),\qquad H_0=\arctan U_0,
\]

\[
m=\mathbb E H_0^2>0.
\]

The initial second preactivation is

\[
Z=\Gamma H_0\sim N(0,m).
\]

Put

\[
G=\arctan Z,\qquad
Y=\frac{\arctan Z}{1+Z^2},
\qquad
\nu=\mathbb E Y^2>0,
\]

\[
\Xi=\Gamma^*Y,
\qquad
d_0=\mathbb E\!\left[q(U_0)^2\Xi^2\right].
\]

The genuine-adjoint identity proves \(d_0>0\), without an independence approximation:

\[
\langle H_0,\Xi\rangle
=
\langle \Gamma H_0,Y\rangle
=
\mathbb E\frac{Z\arctan Z}{1+Z^2}>0.
\]

Thus \(\Xi\ne0\); since \(q(U_0)>0\) almost surely, \(d_0>0\).

Define the positive operator on the left space

\[
\mathcal R
=
mI+
\Gamma M_{q(U_0)^2}\Gamma^*.
\]

Then

\[
\langle Y,\mathcal RY\rangle
=
m\nu+d_0>0.
\tag{25}
\]

Directly integrating (9) at \(t=0\) gives, in \(L^2\) and trace norm,

\[
C(t)=2tG+o(t),
\qquad
D(t)=2tY+o(t),
\]

\[
X(t)=X_0+2t^2\Xi+o(t^2),
\]

\[
Q(t)=2t^2Y\otimes H_0+o(t^2),
\]

\[
U(t)=U_0+2t^2q(U_0)\Xi+o(t^2),
\]

\[
S(t)=Z+2t^2\mathcal RY+o(t^2).
\tag{26}
\]

Therefore,

\[
K^{(1)}(t)=4d_0t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
K^{(2)}(t)=4m\nu t^2+o(t^2),
\]

and, writing \(k_3=\mathbb E G^2>0\),

\[
K^{(3)}(t)
=
k_3+4(d_0+m\nu)t^2+o(t^2).
\]

Hence

\[
K(t)
=
k_3+8(d_0+m\nu)t^2+o(t^2).
\tag{27}
\]

Both hidden velocities are nonzero:

\[
V^{(1)}(t)^2
=
16d_0t^2+o(t^2),
\]

\[
V^{(2)}(t)^2
=
16\|\mathcal RY\|_2^2t^2+o(t^2).
\tag{28}
\]

Their displacements satisfy

\[
\mathbb E|U(t)-U_0|^2
=
4d_0t^4+o(t^4)>0,
\]

\[
\mathbb E|S(t)-Z|^2
=
4\|\mathcal RY\|_2^2t^4+o(t^4)>0.
\tag{29}
\]

Thus every hidden layer learns features macroscopically, and all three parameter blocks have strictly positive time-integrated kernels.

Initially,

\[
\operatorname{Var}(U_0)=1,\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(Z)=m>0.
\]

Wasserstein continuity preserves finite positive variance for sufficiently small time.

For either nondegenerate Gaussian law \(\mu\),

\[
\inf_{a,b}
\int|\arctan z-az-b|^2\,d\mu(z)>0.
\]

Otherwise \(\arctan z=az+b\) almost surely; continuity and full Gaussian support would force equality for every \(z\), which is impossible. The regression residual depends continuously on

\[
\mathbb Ez,\quad \mathbb Ez^2,\quad
\mathbb E\arctan z,\quad
\mathbb Ez\arctan z,\quad
\mathbb E(\arctan z)^2
\]

as long as the variance is positive. Hence both nonlinear residuals remain positive on a short interval.

Finally,

\[
F(0)=0\ne1,
\]

and from (11),

\[
F(t)=2k_3t+o(t),
\qquad
L(t)=1-4k_3t+o(t).
\]

Thus the loss strictly decreases.

Choose \(T_*>0\) small enough that:

- both hidden variances remain positive;
- both nonlinear regression residuals remain positive;
- the positive coefficients in (27)–(29) dominate their remainders.

Then every \(0<T\le T_*\) satisfies properties 3–7, while Sections 3–7 prove properties 1–2 and the full joint diagonal convergence.

Therefore the task has an affirmative resolution. The essential ingredients are the bounded nonlinear activation, the natural coordinate \(u+u^3/3\), the current two-sided Gaussian action state, and the explicit \(\eta_n=n^{-2}\) control of the exact-GD defect.

=== OPERATOR2 ROLLOUT LINE 22095 | 2026-08-27T19:35:20.281Z | assistant | final_answer ===
No. The proposed result is plausible and repairable, but the proof as written is not valid at the requested resolution standard. Its finite-width algebra, joint-GD scaling, and strict small-time feature-learning calculation are correct. The failures are in the construction of the limiting probability/operator state and in the uniform passage of reused-adjoint fields, kernels, and velocities.

After supplying the repairs below, I find no fatal mathematical obstruction to the affirmative theorem. But the original proof should not be presented as complete.

## Audit summary

| Component | Verdict |
|---|---|
| Exact finite-width equations and kernel scaling | Correct |
| Natural-coordinate identity and accumulated GD defect | Correct |
| Fixed-mesh Tensor-Program use | Correct after tightening its statement |
| “Hilbert completion produces probability \(L^2\)-spaces” | False |
| Pointed-action probability/operator construction | Missing but repairable |
| Direct Picard theorem in the stated \(L^2\) space | Invalid as stated, repairable |
| Arbitrary-product action topology | Too strong, must be restricted |
| Path-\(W_2\) convergence argument | Essentially correct |
| Velocity and \(K^{(1)}\) convergence | Incomplete; needs a two-scale tail proof |
| Small-time coefficients and positivity | Correct, but differentiability justification was omitted |
| Final affirmative declaration | Premature as written |

## 1. The Tensor-Program citation: what it does and does not prove

The applicable result is [Yang–Hu, Setup G.2, Theorem G.4, and Assumption L.4](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/yang21c/yang21c-supp.pdf), based on the two-sided master theorem in [Tensor Programs III](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.10685).

Its relevant content is:

- The program is fixed and finite.
- Initial matrices have iid \(N(0,\sigma^2/n)\) entries.
- Initial vector coordinates are iid copies of a fixed jointly Gaussian vector.
- Initial scalar sequences converge almost surely to deterministic limits.
- Both \(Wv\) and \(W^\top v\), with arbitrary reuse, are allowed.
- Every nonlinearity involving vector coordinates and scalar parameters must be jointly pseudo-Lipschitz.
- For every fixed pseudo-Lipschitz test, empirical averages converge almost surely; scalar program variables converge.
- Under this pseudo-Lipschitz hypothesis, no separate rank-stability assumption is imposed. Singular limiting Grams are therefore permitted.

For fixed \(\delta>0\),

\[
Q_k=-2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r\otimes H_r
\]

implies

\[
(\Gamma_n+Q_k)H_k
=
\Gamma_nH_k
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rD_r
 \langle H_r,H_k\rangle_n,
\]

and

\[
(\Gamma_n^\top+Q_k^\top)D_k
=
\Gamma_n^\top D_k
-
2\delta\sum_{r<k}e_rH_r
 \langle D_r,D_k\rangle_n.
\]

Thus a fixed \(T/\delta\)-step Euler scheme is genuinely one finite two-sided Tensor Program. This part of the argument is valid.

The width-dependent readout also fits the theorem exactly: write

\[
C_{0,n}=\frac1nR_{0,n},
\qquad R_{0,n}\sim N(0,I_n),
\]

and treat \(1/n\to0\) as an initial scalar and \((r,a)\mapsto ar\) as a pseudo-Lipschitz Nonlin operation.

What Theorem G.4 does not give is:

- continuous-time convergence;
- convergence for \(T/\eta_n\to\infty\) program steps;
- a countable current action state;
- a bounded operator on probability \(L^2\)-spaces;
- path-\(W_2\) convergence.

Those require separate arguments.

## 2. The probability/action construction in the proof is invalid as written

The sentence

> “Quotient the program fields by zero \(L^2\)-norm and complete them. This produces two probability \(L^2\)-spaces.”

is false. Hilbert completion produces an abstract Hilbert space, not a probability space with pointwise operations. On such a Hilbert space,

\[
J(X),\qquad \arctan(MH),\qquad Cq(S)
\]

are not defined.

### Repair

Construct one countable, sequential, two-sorted program in stages. At each stage include:

- rational linear combinations;
- \(\Gamma v\) and \(\Gamma^\top w\);
- the required moment operations;
- the fixed maps \(J,\arctan,q,\Theta\);
- a countable family of rational compactly supported Lipschitz cylinder maps.

Dovetailing gives one infinite instruction list such that every finite prefix is a legal finite Tensor Program. Apply Theorem G.4 separately to every finite prefix and every test from a countable convergence-determining class. A countable intersection preserves probability one.

The limiting finite-dimensional distributions are consistent: extending a prefix leaves every previous finite-width variable unchanged, so uniqueness of deterministic bounded-Lipschitz limits forces agreement on overlaps. Kolmogorov extension then produces probability spaces

\[
(\Omega_R,\mu_R),\qquad (\Omega_L,\mu_L).
\]

Bounded cylinder functions are dense in the resulting \(L^2\)-spaces. Indeed, finite-coordinate conditional expectations converge in \(L^2\), and compactly supported rational piecewise-linear functions are dense under every finite-dimensional Borel law.

For every program field \(v\),

\[
\|\Gamma_nv_n\|_n
\le \|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}\|v_n\|_n.
\]

A Gaussian net estimate gives

\[
\Pr(\|\Gamma_n\|_{\rm op}>10)\le 2e^{-cn}.
\]

Therefore, almost surely eventually,

\[
\|Z^{\Gamma v}\|_{L^2_L}
\le 10\|Z^v\|_{L^2_R}.
\]

This makes

\[
\Gamma_0Z^v:=Z^{\Gamma v}
\]
